Real Madrid manager Jose Mourinho expressed his "tremendous pride" after tennis legend Rafael Nadal was officially elected to the club's Board of Directors with 99.5 per cent of the vote. Praising the 22-time Grand Slam champion's character beyond his sporting achievements, Mourinho welcomed one of Madrid's most famous supporters into the hierarchy.

Real Madrid head coach Jose Mourinho has paid glowing tribute to Rafael Nadal after the tennis legend was officially ratified as a new member of the club's Board of Directors. Speaking after Los Blancos' latest La Liga fixture, the Portuguese tactician emphasised that having the 22-time Grand Slam champion in the club's hierarchy carries deep personal meaning.

"And I also wanted to say that, as the Real Madrid coach, it is a great honour for me that one of my board members is not just the athlete Rafa Nadal but the person Rafael Nadal," Mourinho stated. "It is a tremendous source of pride for me."

Nadal's appointment was confirmed during Real Madrid's Extraordinary General Assembly, held both in person and online at Ciudad Real Madrid's basketball pavilion. Out of 1,713 votes cast by representative members under Article 46 of the club's bylaws, 1,704 voted in favour, with just four against and five abstentions—a decisive 99.5 per cent approval rating.

Greeted with a standing ovation, the former world number one pledged to approach his new role with humility. "It is a great responsibility to be part of the Board of Directors of Real Madrid," Nadal said. "I am very aware of the responsibility because Real Madrid is a global brand. It is an infinite responsibility, and I take it on with the hope of learning and contributing so that this club continues to be the best in the world."

Mourinho's admiration for Nadal's values off the court mirrored the sentiments of club president Florentino Perez, who chaired the single-item assembly. An honorary member of Real Madrid since 2011, Nadal retired with 92 individual titles, two Olympic gold medals and five Davis Cups alongside his 22 Grand Slams.

"Those of us who have had the opportunity to know him understand that his greatness goes far beyond his successes and countless titles," Perez said. "Rafa is a person who has earned the respect and affection of millions of people because of his personality, his behaviour on and off the courts, and his unwavering commitment to the values that make sports great."

With Nadal now formally installed on the board alongside Perez and working in the same institution as Mourinho, both the president and the tennis icon underlined their shared ambition to keep Real Madrid at the summit of world sport.

"I will work to ensure that things continue as they are: a successful club that remains so for years to come," Nadal concluded before signing off with "Hala Madrid!"

#2874. Colts release starting QB Daniel Jones less than a year after extending him

Colts release starting QB Daniel Jones less than a year after extending him originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Daniel Jones played so well in his first 10 games with the Indianapolis Colts that he entered the MVP conversation.

His last 11 games were so bad, he lost his job.

On Friday, less than 24 hours after yet another abysmal offensive performance in a 26-3 loss at Houston, Colts coach Shane Steichen said the team was releasing Jones and turning to Anthony Richardson as the starter when it faces the New York Giants next week. It's the second time in three years Jones has been released in November, the first coming after he sought and received his release from the Giants in 2024.

Jones then joined the Minnesota Vikings practice squad before signing a one-year deal with the Colts in March 2025 and a two-year deal worth $88 million earlier this year. This time, though, Indy — not Jones — made the call.

"This was a decision that was made for, you know, the best interests of both parties," Steichen said, explaining Jones had not asked for his release. "It's just where we're at in our season. That's just the situation we're at right now."

For the Colts (3-8), it's another key move in what already has been a tumultuous season.

Team co-owner Carlie Irsay-Gordon fired general manager Chris Ballard last month, and Indy then traded four key players — All-Pro guard Quenton Nelson, Pro Bowl defensive tackle DeForest Buckner, Pro Bowl receiver Keenan Allen and receiver Josh Downs — before this month's trading deadline. Nelson and Buckner were captains and longtime locker room mainstays while Allen and Downs were Indy's top two receivers.

Now they're cutting ties with Jones, who was going to be a $46.8 million salary cap hit next season. Instead, the Colts will save about $11.6 million, as they give Richardson, the No. 4 overall pick in the 2023 draft, a second chance to prove himself. Steichen said he had spoken with Irsay-Gordon before announcing the move but that he made the final decision.

"I think there's a lot of factors, I don't think it was just on one person," Steichen said. "Obviously, the quarterback takes a lot of the blame, obviously, that's the business we're in. But things, you know, just didn't work out."

Jones started his Colts career fast, leading them to an 8-2 mark on one of the most productive offenses in league history through 10 games. But Jones wasn't the same after breaking a bone in his left leg last November and struggled mightily this season after returning from last December's season-ending torn right Achilles tendon.

His completion percentage, 58.4%, and passer rating, 65.4, rank among the lowest of all NFL starters. He also struggled with turnovers, throwing 13 interceptions and losing three fumbles. The increasingly poor performances had fans calling for a change.

Jones followed his season-worst passer rating of 41.5 in Sunday's 24-22 victory over the one-win Miami Dolphins with his second worst single-game rating this year, 45.9, on Thursday night. He was 12 of 27 for 144 yards with one interception and was sacked for a safety.

Still, Steichen fully supported Jones, insisting he gave Indy the best chance to win.

"I can't say enough good things about Daniel, just the ultimate competitor, ultimate teammate," Steichen said. "The toughness he brought to our football team — I mean the guy played on a broken leg for us last year for three or four games, snapped his Achilles, came back 8 1/2 months later and gave this organization everything he had. I couldn't be more thankful for the time I had with him."

Richardson replaced Jones in the fourth quarter Thursday. He was 0 for 2 with one carry for 2 yards.

Now, the guy who has twice requested to be traded since losing the quarterback battle to Jones during training camp in 2025, will take over. The former Florida star has struggled with accuracy, posting a career completion percentage of just 50.6% while throwing 11 TD passes and 13 interceptions.

Richardson also has battled injuries, playing in just 15 of the Colts' 34 games when he was the starter. This year, he's 2 of 4 for 8 yards in three games.

But now the guy who was once billed as the future face of the franchise will get a new opportunity. Second-year quarterback Riley Leonard is the only other quarterback on Indy's active roster.

"I talked to Anthony today, I told him, obviously, he'll be the starter versus the Giants," Steichen said. "There's, obviously, a world in which Riley could play as well so we'll evaluate that each and every week throughout the season."

#2873. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

#2872. Retirees face a looming deadline for required withdrawals — missing it could trigger a tax penalty

Every year, millions of Americans must take required withdrawals from retirement accounts by Dec. 31.

Amid the busy holiday season, it's easy to miss the year-end deadline for required minimum distributions — a mistake that can trigger a hefty IRS penalty, experts said.

"One of the most common RMD mistakes is waiting until the last minute," said certified financial planner Alex Canellopoulos, director of wealth at Vista Capital Partners in Portland, Oregon.

Most retirees must start RMDs from pretax accounts at age 73. The withdrawal is based on the previous year's account balances on Dec. 31, your age and an IRS "life expectancy factor."

The first annual RMD deadline is April 1 of the year after you turn 73, and future withdrawals are due by Dec. 31. If you wait until April 1 for the first one, you'll need two RMDs that year.

RMDs also apply to certain inherited IRAs if the original IRA owner reached RMD age before their death.

In 2023, more than 14.8 million taxpayers age 70 or older had a traditional individual retirement account, according to the latest IRS data. The average year-end market value of the accounts was about $330,000.

Deferring RMDs until the last minute raises the likelihood of mistakes, such as overlooking certain retirement accounts or making withdrawal calculation errors, Canellopoulos said.

Some 7% of Vanguard investors at RMD age missed their yearly withdrawal in 2025, up slightly from 6.7% in 2024, according to a Vanguard analysis of about 400,000 RMD-age retirees with IRA balances published in December 2025.

Among those investors, the average missed RMD was $10,500 in 2025, Vanguard told CNBC.

Investors who don't take RMDs by the annual deadline may owe an excise tax to the IRS, worth up to 25% of the amount they should have withdrawn. For example, the excise tax could be up to $2,625 for a $10,500 missed RMD.

If investors miss a required withdrawal, they could reduce the penalty to 10% if they "timely corrected" the RMD within two years and file Form 5329, according to the IRS.

In some cases, the IRS could waive the penalty entirely due to "reasonable error" if investors take "reasonable steps" to fix the shortfall, according to the agency. Investors can file Form 5329 with a letter of explanation to request the waiver.

"If you don't ask, you're not going to get it," said certified public accountant Tim Steffen, director of advanced planning at financial services firm Baird. "I've never heard of anybody being denied the waiver."

Investors who give to charity may be skipping a specific donation strategy that can reduce or eliminate their annual RMDs, according to Lawrence Pon, a CFP and CPA based in Redwood City, California.

They can use a so-called qualified charitable distribution, or QCD, Pon said.

A QCD has a dual tax benefit: It doesn't count as taxable income and may also satisfy at least part of an investor's yearly RMD, Pon said.

The move requires a direct transfer from an investor's pretax IRA to an eligible nonprofit organization. They can donate up to $111,000 in 2026. While QCD eligibility begins at age 70½, transfers won't start counting toward RMDs until an investor reaches their RMD age.

Generally, taxpayers only get a tax break for large charitable donations if they itemize deductions on their tax returns. But QCDs still provide a tax benefit for charitable gifts when investors use the standard deduction.

QCDs "provide a real benefit if you are not itemizing [tax deductions]," said Pon.

While there's no tax deduction for QCDs, the transfer won't increase a taxpayer's adjusted gross income, or AGI. Raising AGI can cause other tax consequences, such as higher premiums for Medicare Part B and Part D.

#2871. Notre Dame’s night could have been a lot different against the SMU Mustangs

I'm not sure how many times I used the word "odd" or "weird" while talking about Notre Dame's 24-3 win over the SMU Mustangs, but I know it was a lot. You'll have to forgive me — it was just one of those days.

There was something like 23 games this week with a ranked versus unranked matchup. Most people would assume that meant it would be a boring week of college football, but I've lived long enough to have seen seasons like 2007 — so I know things can get strange. And guess what… it did.

Saturday wasn't a BLOOD WEEK but it had enough upsets and close games to make it a wild day, which makes it something like a wizard's spell that was cast all over the country.

Notre Dame well could have blown the doors off of SMU, and I understand the disappointment that some fans may have in this performance. The thing is though… your disappointment is dead ass wrong.

The Irish covered the spread, and pretty much put this game away with the first drive of the 2nd half — so where was the game pressure that should exist for your angst to be anything more than bring a spoiled little kid with a bag full of Snickers but bitches about not having any Butterfingers?

This entire game can be summed up by the first drive of the game when we saw a 70 yard run by Kendrick Raphael stopped by a hustling Leonard Moore, a botched FG attempt by SMU, and an injury to Luke Talich. So it wasn't the drive itself, but how Notre Dame responded to it… which was a fumble, a defensive 3 and out, and then a 99 yard TD drive that ended with a rushing touchdown by a tight end (Cooper Flanagan).

Yeah man — this night was weird. Did you want it to be weirder and have it be a closer game or a loss? Then quit your bitching. I don't care what Wake Forest did to this SMU team last week, because frankly, the Irish would wipe the turf with the Demon Deacons. So if that thought about Wake Forest haunted you all night — I feel sorry for you.

The Irish finished its first undefeated home season in the Marcus Freeman era, covered the spread, improved to 10-1 for the season, and will likely move up the CFP rankings. I mean… did you want the Irish to win 56-0 and push hard for a top 4 slot? I thought most of you were rooting for a home playoff game and a bye?

Yes, this night could have been much different for the better, and for the worse. Instead, we got a comfortable win with one more game to go to officially make the college football playoff.

It's fine. You're fine. Notre Dame is fine.

#2870. JJ Redick hopeful Austin Reaves will return in next two games

#2869. Future Eagles Fits: Five interior defensive linemen to watch in 2027 NFL draft

Future Eagles Fits: Five interior defensive linemen to watch in 2027 NFL draft originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Welcome back to Future Eagles Fits, our weekly deep dive into the prospects who could eventually find their way to Philadelphia.

Every week, we're going inside the tape and looking at prospects through an Eagles-centric lens — breaking down the athletic profile, positional versatility and scheme fit that could ultimately determine whether a player makes sense in Philly. Along the way, we'll examine how prospects are being evaluated across the league as draft boards begin to take shape and conversations are had with league representatives across the scouting landscape.

For the Eagles, the interior of their defensive line has quickly become a position to monitor as the organization begins to look ahead. A first and second round pick in return for Jordan Davis is outstanding additional draft capital to work with, but it also leaves a significant piece of the defensive front to replace moving forward. Jalen Carter remains the centerpiece of the group, while Moro Ojomo, Ty Robinson, and Byron Young will have opportunities to take on larger roles within the rotation. Still, adding another young, explosive presence to the interior could go a long way toward maintaining the physicality and disruptive ability that has become such an important part of the Eagles' defensive identity.

The good news? It's an intriguing class up front with several players who offer unique skill sets that could make sense for what the Eagles are looking to add. Whether it's finding another high-end interior pass rusher, adding a versatile defensive lineman who can align up and down the front, or bringing in a bigger-bodied presence who can control the line of scrimmage, there are legitimate options to consider.

While there is still a ton of football left to be played and the entirety of the pre-draft process still to play out — which will alter boards — here are five interior defensive linemen to know in the upcoming class — two potential Day 1 targets, two Day 2 prospects and one Day 3 option who could provide some additional juice up front.

Day 1: Xavier Gilliam, Tennessee | 6'2″, 307 lbs (verified measurements)

If you're looking for an interior presence who can immediately change the way an opposing offense has to approach its protection schemes, it starts with Gilliam in this class.

A former transfer from Penn State, Gilliam has developed into one of the most explosive defensive players in the entire country this season and he's currently a top-10 player on my personal board. Below is a quick peek at the type of pop he provides up front.

At nearly 310 pounds, Gilliam has an outstanding combination of lower-body explosiveness, strength and initial quickness off the snap. He doesn't need much space to generate momentum, and his ability to explode out of his stance allows him to create immediate problems for guards attempting to establish angles.

There's a level of violence to his game that really stands out, as well. He plays with outstanding energy, consistently attacks the line of scrimmage and has the type of first-step quickness that can make even well-positioned offensive linemen uncomfortable. He's not simply a defensive tackle who occupies space and looks to disrupt the run game, he has legitimate pass-rush juice and the athletic profile to create negative plays without needing a blitz or additional help from the second level.

For Philly, he and Carter would be a true nightmare to counter due to their explosiveness up front. Add in Ojomo – we'll see if they do in fact extend him – and all three would give the Eagles an outstanding foundation along the line of scrimmage. If you're looking to invest first-round capital in an interior defensive lineman, finding someone who can make an impact in both phases of the game is a pretty good place to start. He's one of the most exciting defensive prospects in the entire class.

Day 1: Devan Thompkins, Alabama | 6'5″, 290 lbs (verified measurements)

Gilliam is the explosive interior disruptor, but Thompkins might be the most versatile defensive lineman in the entire draft.

A transfer from USC to Alabama, Thompkins has the opportunity to be the first interior/edge hybrid selected next April. At 290 pounds, he brings a unique physical profile to the position, and I'm not sure there's another player in this class who offers quite the same flexibility when it comes to moving around a defensive front.

The biggest selling point with Thompkins is that he doesn't necessarily need to be confined to one particular alignment. He can line up directly across from a guard and attack the interior, work outside an offensive tackle, or even operate as a wide-nine defender outside the tight end. It's an incredibly valuable skill set for any defensive coordinator who wants to manufacture different looks, create favorable matchups and keep opposing offensive lines guessing.

There's real power behind his game, too, which matters for young players making the transition to the pro game. It can't all be finesse, and Thompkins has herculean strength to generate movement at the point of attack, and his ability to attack from different alignments makes him particularly difficult to counter from down to down. He has the athleticism to challenge offensive tackles on the outside, but also possesses the strength and power to create problems when working inside against guards.

What makes his evaluation particularly interesting is the number of different ways an NFL coaching staff could utilize him. Want to kick him inside and create a mismatch against a guard? Sure. Line him up outside the tackle and let him use his length and athleticism to get after the quarterback? That's another option. He's the type of player who gives a defensive coordinator the freedom to be creative with personnel and alignment without sacrificing physicality, and for the Eagles, that versatility could be especially appealing. Philadelphia has traditionally valued defensive linemen who can move around the front and create problems from multiple alignments. With Carter and Ojomo already providing interior options, Thompkins could give the Eagles another player who allows them to change their defensive presentation depending on the opponent.

Day 2: A.J. Holmes Jr, Texas Tech | 6'2″, 300 lbs, 32 ⅜ arm (verified measurements)

Holmes is a name whose draft stock has continued to trend upward throughout the process, and there's a lot to like about what he brings to the interior defensive line.

At 300 pounds, he has a compact, powerful frame that allows him to hold his ground against offensive linemen while also providing pass-rush upside.

He's capable of winning against individual blocks with his power using his upper-body strength to create separation and disrupt the timing of opposing run concepts, but he's not a player who needs to rely exclusively on athleticism to make an impact. There's a physical element to his game that allows him to compete at the point of attack and create problems for offensive linemen who aren't prepared for his initial punch.

He's also shown the ability to provide something as a pass rusher, which is particularly important when evaluating interior defensive linemen in this range. Holmes has enough quickness to attack gaps and create pressure from the inside, giving him a pathway to contribute on passing downs rather than simply being a rotational run defender. His ability to generate disruption without requiring a favorable matchup is an important part of his evaluation, and he's excelled so far this year totaling 11 sacks through 10 games for the Red Raiders.

There are still areas to develop, particularly when it comes to consistently anchoring against combination blocks and maintaining his positioning when offenses devote additional attention to him, but that's part of the appeal of finding a player here on Day 2.

For Philly, Holmes would provide another young body who can compete for snaps in the middle of the defensive line. Established pieces are present, but continuing to add depth and competition to the rotation is important, particularly after moving Davis. Holmes has the ability to contribute against the run while offering pass-rush upside, making him an intriguing developmental option who could eventually grow into a larger role.

Day 2: Jordan Hall, Georgia | 6'4″, 320 lbs, 35 ¼ arm (verified measurements)

The Bulldogs have consistently produced NFL-caliber defensive linemen, and Hall is another player whose combination of size, length, and physicality makes him an intriguing prospect in the class.

The bottom line for the Georgia product – he's an immovable force in the ground game.

There's a level of natural strength to his game that makes it extremely difficult for offensive linemen to create movement against him. He has the length to control a rep early on, size to occupy space, the lower-body strength to anchor against double teams and the physicality to make life difficult for opposing offensive lines attempting to establish their running game. He's not necessarily going to win every snap by making a spectacular play in the backfield, but his ability to screw his cleats into the grass and prevent offenses from creating movement at the point of attack is what makes him so appealing.

And sometimes, that's exactly what a defense needs.

There's also a natural fit when considering the Eagles' recent investment in Georgia defenders. Roseman has consistently shown an appreciation for the program's defensive talent, and Hall would give the Eagles another opportunity to bring in a player from a pipeline they know well.

After moving Davis, adding another big-bodied presence who can control the middle of the defensive line would make plenty of sense. Carter and Ojomo offer different skill sets, and Hall could complement those players by providing a more traditional interior run defender who can eat up space and make it difficult for opposing offenses to establish their identity on the ground.

Day 3: Jide Abasiri, USC | 6'4″, 281 lbs (verified measurements)

I hope Abasiri ends up at an All-Star game in the pre-draft process for another evaluation exposure as he could end up being one of the more intriguing value selections in the middle to late stages of the draft.

A young prospect who just turned 21 years old, Abasiri has all the physical tools NFL teams drool over when going through the evaluation process. At 6-foot-4 and 285 pounds, he brings excellent length to the interior defensive line and has shown enough flashes throughout the season to suggest there's a lot more potential to unlock as he continues developing.

The first thing that stands out is his physical profile, as Abasiri has the type of length that can create immediate problems for opposing offensive linemen, particularly when he's able to extend and establish his hands to keep blockers from getting into his frame. For a younger player, that combination of size and length is an appealing starting point, especially when considering how much development can take place within ideal coaching surroundings in the NFL.

At this stage of the process, consistency is going to be an important part of his evaluation. There's a difference between flashing the ability to win at the college level and consistently executing those techniques against NFL-caliber offensive linemen. Abasiri still has room to develop his overall game, particularly when it comes to translating those physical tools into more consistent production, and it's a main reason why he's in this range to begin with.

Right now, he's someone who could land anywhere from the late third round into the early-mid portion of Day 3, depending on how the rest of the evaluation process unfolds. His age, physical makeup and flashes of disruption give him an appealing developmental profile, and a strong finish to the season followed by a good showing throughout the pre-draft process will absolutely help his stock continue to climb.

For the Eagles, they don't necessarily need every defensive lineman it drafts to come in and immediately command a significant snap share. Finding younger players with legitimate physical upside who can develop behind established veterans is an important part of maintaining a productive defensive front over time, and Abasiri has the length, athleticism and developmental runway to make him an intriguing option later in the draft.

#2866. Rob Pelinka on Lakers: 'We’ll be active at the trade deadline'

The Los Angeles Lakers sit on top of the Western Conference at 14-3, with the third-best offense in the NBA — behind an MVP-level start to the season from Luka Doncic — and a solid defense. Walker Kessler has been exactly what the Lakers hoped for as a big in the paint, and their moves for role players that better fit around Doncic — Matisse Thybulle, Sandro Mamukelashvili, Quentin Grimes, Quentin Grimes — have largely worked out.

Lakers general manager Rob Pelinka has seen it, too, and thinks this team has a deep playoff run in them. Which is why he said in an interview on the Lakers pregame show that the team would be "active" at the trade deadline (hat tip Real GM).

"We'll be active at the trade deadline to shore up things that we see. We think this team has a real chance to make a run, so we want to give the group as much talent as we can. Don't want to get too far over our skis with a hot start, because you don't want to get big-headed, but clearly the way it's gone so far, it's been great, and we hope it continues."

Active is a broad term. The Lakers will be active talking to teams, whether they make a deal will depend on the players available, and the price.

The Lakers' most critical need is to improve at backup center behind Kessler. Kevon Looney has largely fallen out of the rotation, leaving the Lakers playing small ball with Sandro Mamukelashvili or Jarred Vanderbilt at the five, or leaning into two-way big Carson Cooper. There may be better options available.

The Lakers don't have draft picks of note to trade, but they have players, and while they are hard-capped at the first apron, they are a little more than $8 million below that line and have room to work.

#2865. ‘What's different? My confidence': Joe Milton used bye week to master offense

'What's different? My confidence': Joe Milton used bye week to master offense originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The bye week gave Joe Milton a chance to do stuff he really had to do.

Milton made his first NFL start after just 23 days and 10 practices with the Eagles and it went about as expected in a 19-11 loss to the Giants, the Eagles' seventh straight loss.

Two weeks later, Milton feels like a different man.

Thanks to the bye week, he's had two weeks to devote to learning an offense that he just didn't have time to learn before his first start.

It's still not a lot of time, but 37 days is a lot different than 23 days.

"What's different for me now? My confidence," Milton said after the Eagles returned to practice Wednesday. "Just confidence in knowing this offense and what these coaches and my teammates around me are instilling in me and talking to me about.

"And just my reason to ask some questions. I'm great at asking questions, and I ask Parks (Frazier) and Sean (Mannion) a lot of questions. And I don't think I can ask (more) questions than I already do. I think I do a great job at that. So just yeah, just my confidence for sure.

"I feel more comfortable than I did last week for sure. Just the verbiage and everything that we needed to do … to solve the problem and fix it."

Milton wasn't awful in the Giants loss considering the circumstances. He didn't have DeVonta Smith or Dontayvion Wicks and the offensive line was unable to protect him, on top of his lack of reps in Mannion's offense.

He'll make his second career start Sunday at the Linc against the Steelers, who are 6-3 but ranked 30th in pass defense, worse even than the Eagles.

For the record, the last Eagles quarterback to lose his first two career starts was Nick Foles in 2012.

For Milton, the bye week couldn't have come at a better time.

"It was very helpful just because I was able to communicate (with) the coaches (about) what we need to do better and what we need to get better at and where I need to be better and just echoing that and fixing the protections for myself. Learning it, studying it more, being able to pick up the blitzes whenever they show up, and just really just understanding the verbiage that we're pronouncing and saying here."

Milton completed 18 for 34 passes for 169 yards against the Giants with a long gainer of 20 yards, no touchdowns or interceptions. He also ran nine times for 64 yards and ran for a two-point conversion. The Eagles are last in the NFL in total offense and 30th in passing offense and scoring.

One thing Milton did during the bye was ask tons of questions. That's big for him, a good way for him to learn. He's big on questions.

"I ask questions every day," he said. "It could be the same question. I just want to make sure I heard correctly and I took down the right note. I'm big on writing notes. And my coaches do a great job of just like overly talking, just constantly mentioning the same thing. And I mean, you kind of hear it every day and all throughout the week, so it kind of gets stuck in your brain."

"Just what do I need to do on this play?" he said. "Like, what is what is the quarterback job specifically on this play? That goes for every play. Like, what do you exactly want me to do on this play? And if there's anything different, just tell me now."

Milton will have Smith and Wicks back Sunday, so that should help.

"Those two guys coming back obviously helps out the team," he said. "For me to throw to those guys today was amazing. I mean, shoot, I couldn't stop smiling for real if you ask me."

The Eagles haven't won a game in two months so there hasn't been a lot of smiling going on around the team facility.

A more settled, more prepared Milton could go a long way toward ending this ugly seven-game losing streak.

"I know what I need to know as far as this offense and whatever goes on in this offense," he said. "So I think I'm in the right place and the right time as far as where I'm at with this offense.

"It's not rocket science. Football is football, no matter where you go. It's all about X's and O's and just knowing football. Coverage is going to be the coverage, a play is going to be a play, and if it's a run game, just hand it off to the running back."

#2863. Eagles-Steelers matchups to watch in Week 11

Eagles-Steelers matchups to watch in Week 11 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-7) are hosting the Pittsburgh Steelers (6-3) at the Linc on Sunday afternoon at 1.

The biggest move Howie Roseman made before the trade deadline was sending Jordan Davis to the Jacksonville Jaguars for a first-rounder and a second-rounder. So this will be the Eagles' first game without their run-stuffing nose tackle and they're facing a team that is averaging 4.4 yards per carry on the ground — the fourth-highest average in the NFL.

The Steelers are 14th in overall rushing but have been efficient this season. Their ground game starts with running back Jaylen Warren, who has 126 carries for 719 yards and 2 touchdowns this season. That 5.7 yards-per-carry average is the best in the NFL among qualified players. Of Warren's 719 yards this season, 533 of them have come after contact. He's averaging 4.2 yards after contact per attempt, which is the fourth-highest average in the NFL. So it'll be important for the Eagles to wrap up in this game.

The good news for the Eagles is that they had their best run defense game of the season before the bye week against the Giants, allowing just 80 yards on the ground. It was the first time all season they held their opponent under 100 rushing yards.

Eagles LBs and S vs. Pat Freiermuth, Darnell Washington

The Steelers' leading receiver this season is DK Metcalf, who has 432 yards and 2 touchdowns on 35 catches. He is also leading the team in targets with 70. So maybe this is another good week to have Quinyon Mitchell travel with a top receiver. But the Steelers also have two tight ends who each see a ton of action in Pat Freiermuth and Darnell Washington.

Freiermuth: 38 targets, 27 catches, 270 yards, 5 TDs

Washington: 31 targets, 20 catches, 259 yards, 3 TDs

The Steelers have 15 passing touchdowns this season and 8 of them come from these two players. So the Eagles have to be aware of them in the red zone.

The Eagles have actually been very good against tight ends this season. They have allowed 38 catches for 386 yards and 3 touchdowns to opposing tight ends this season. Just two teams (Titans and Jaguars) have allowed fewer yards to opposing tight ends. But this will be a big test for their safeties and linebackers, especially if Zack Baun (back) is unable to play.

Aaron Rodgers is one of the greatest QBs in NFL history but he's obviously past his prime. Rodgers will turn 43 on Dec. 2, but he's having an OK season. He has completed 59.9% of his passes for 2,014 yards with 15 touchdowns and 6 interceptions. But he has already been sacked 26 times in nine games. The only QBs who have been sacked more this year are Deshaun Watson (37), Justin Herbert (33), Geno Smith (29) and Bryce Young (29).

Despite that, the Steelers are allowing a pressure rate of just 31.4%, which is the seventh-best QB pressure rate in the NFL. The 49ers are tops at 27.9%. While Rodgers is getting sacked quite a bit, he's also getting the ball out quickly. His average time to throw is 2.75 seconds, which is eighth in the NFL.

The Steelers are going to be without starting left guard Mason McCormick, who suffered an ankle injury in the loss to the Bengals.

The Eagles' defense finally broke out before the bye week with six sacks against the Giants after having just 10 entering the game. But with 16 sacks, they still rank 28th in the NFL this season and their pressure rate of 32.6% ranks 26th.

The plan this season was to have third-round pick Markel Bell sit and learn behind all-time great Lane Johnson but that plan got thrown into a blender earlier this year when Johnson abruptly retired. While Bell has had some rough moments, it'll be his job for the rest of the season. That became abundantly clear when the Eagles released Fred Johnson late last week. In six games, Bell has given up 5 sacks and 19 pressures. There have been ups and downs as the Miami product adjusts to the NFL.

This won't be an easy week. T.J. Watt leads the Steelers with 7 1/2 sacks and is second in pressures on the team with 23. (Don't sleep on Alex Highsmith or Nick Herbig; this is a dangerous group of edge rushers.) Watt, 32, is a future Hall of Famer and a former Defensive Player of the Year who is fourth on the active sack list with 122.5. He's behind just Von Miller (139.5), Cameron Jordan (132) and Myles Garrett (128.5). Oh yeah, and he plays most of his snaps on the defensive left, which sets up this matchup against Bell. The Eagles will need to help the rookie in this game.

Before the bye week, Saquon Barkley was dealing with hamstring and back injuries that were limiting him. Barkley looked good in limited action against the Cowboys but the last two games had 26 carries for 52 yards. Barkley has looked good when healthy this season but he's also running behind an offensive line that has really struggled this season. He'll see a tough test in Week 11.

The Steelers this season have the NFL's No. 3-ranked run defense. They have given up just 83.2 yards per game on the ground in 2026 and have held opponents to 43.3 rushing yards per game over their last three games. They have given up just 0.71 yards before contact per attempt.

DeVonta Smith vs. Asante Samuel Jr., Jamel Dean

In the Steelers' Week 10 loss to the Bengals, they had no answer for Jamar Chase, who ended up with 11 catches on 14 targets for 231 yards. Based PFF's data, Chase caught a pass against six different Steelers defenders but did most of his damage against their top two corners:

Chase vs. Samuel Jr.: 4 targets, 2 caches, 96 yards

Chase vs. Dean: 5 targets, 4 catches, 53 yards

The Steelers have been playing Jalen Ramsey at nickel corner and safety. He's no longer a full-time outside cornerback, so we won't see him on Smith in this game. At one point, the Steelers had a cornerback who would have had a better shot against Smith but they were unable to reach a contract agreement with Joey Porter Jr. and traded him to the Cowboys in late September.

Smith has missed the last three games with a hamstring injury but if he's healthy in this one, it could be a big opportunity for him in his first game with starting QB Joe Milton.

#2862. Brad Stevens talks Mitchell Robinson, Celtics’ start, and more

SACRAMENTO — Brad Stevens took a lot of heat this offseason for pulling the trigger on the trade that sent Jaylen Brown to the Philadelphia 76ers and ended a near-decade-long partnership with Jayson Tatum.

And, though it's too soon to tell, the Celtics are 11-4, have the second-best net rating in the NBA, the second-best defense, and the sixth-best offense. Paul George, the team's biggest offseason acqusition, has been generally awesome. The team's significant depth has been on full display regularly.

Still, Stevens said it's too soon to judge who this team really is, and that he normally waits until mid-December to make those kinds of evaluations.

"I've been encouraged by a lot of things," Stevens said. "I think we've still got a lot to figure out to be the best version of ourselves. But usually, I'm probably another couple of weeks or a month away from getting too deep into, okay, this is who we really are. Yet, I think we have played some good basketball. We've shown ourselves capable of it. We've gotten good play from a lot of different guys, and the team has really tried to buy in, I think, well, to what the coaches are asking them to do on both sides of the ball."

Paul George has been one of the brightest spots of the Celtics' early-season success. George has been tremendous defensively, and he's also averaging 19.9 points, 5.5 rebounds, and 3.7 assists per game, while shooting 46.5% from the field and 42.3% from three. He's had two games with at least 35 points and has been one of the team's most reliable defenders.

"Paul's been a good fit," Stevens said. "Paul's a really good player…. You don't come with those accolades if you're not able to impact the game. Everybody talks about what he can do on offense, and I think the minute he stepped on the court with our guys defensively, our guys, a lot of them were kind of taken aback, even though playing against him all these years, with how good he is, how active he is, how smart he is, how aware he is, and he's just really had a good impact on the group. He's just been a joy to be around. He's been great."

The Celtics' other marquee offseason acquisition, Mitchell Robinson, hasn't transitioned as seamlessly as George. Robinson has the worst on-off rating on the Celtics at -30.1 (that number measures the difference in net rating when a player is on the floor versus off of it). The historically prolific rebounder is also averaging the fewest rebounds per game (5.4) of his career.

"You can tell it's obviously a transition for him, and that's not all that atypical for guys, and so again, it's so early that we'll see how this all plays out," Stevens said. "But I trust that what he does when he's at his best will translate because it always has, right? And I think that getting him to the rim on both sides of the floor, getting him to be in that area where he can be most impactful, [is] something that we'll continue to work on — continue to put him in position where, hopefully, he can soar with his strengths."

Then, there's rookie Chris Cenac Jr., who began the year with the Maine Celtics but has found himself in the Celtics' rotation over the past two games. Cenac, who was drafted 27th overall, tallied 6 points and 6 rebounds in 17 minutes in the Celtics' loss to the Nuggets on Wednesday night.

"He's been solid," Stevens said. "I'm really encouraged. I really like him. I think he's got a great work ethic. He's obviously got physical tools. He's got a good mind for the game. He's gonna be good, right? I do think that with each step, there are different expectations. So he's in that honeymoon phase of, well, he did one good thing and it looks awesome, and wow. And then your expectations grow and grow, and it's just like we've seen with Jordan and Hugo and Baylor. Once you taste it, once you get a little feeling, then the expectation is to be even better, and we see that all the way up and down our roster. All of our guys, for the most part, have been through that. But I'm encouraged by the start. It's hard not to be, and I think the other part of it is he does have the physical ability to continue to raise his level in big moments."

One of the other interesting aspects of the roster is the logjam at the wing position; Sam Hauser (20.3 minutes), Baylor Scheierman (19.4 minutes), Jordan Walsh (15.7 minutes), and Hugo Gonzalez (12.9 minutes) have all seen significant minutes, but no one player in that group has pulled away to play the majority of the minutes.

Each brings something different to the table; Hauser (46.8% from three) and Scheierman (42.2% from three) have been the biggest threats from beyond the arc, while Gonzalez (7.1 rebounds/36 minutes) has been the best rebounder of the bunch. From the wings, Walsh has probably been the team's best on-ball defender and its most consistent starter, with 10 starts in 15 games.

But the players have seemed interchangeable at times, and no one has clearly emerged as the best player in the pack.

"I said at the start of the year, the biggest challenge for the coaching staff is the wing differentiation," Stevens said. "That's been the hardest part, and I think they've managed it beautifully, because nobody has really differentiated themselves, and they're all good players. And so that's just kind of the hard part about having depth, which in this league you need now. It's really important, but it also comes with the challenges of — not everybody's going to get exactly what they want on every given night. Is it easier to have seven or eight guys that you know are going to play every night? Yeah, until two of them get hurt, then you're in trouble, right? And so that's part of the challenge in this day and age with how the league is going and how hard it is to play the full 82, and certainly game to game with what's required on both ends."

As the team continues to improve on both ends, Stevens is generally encouraged by what he's seen.

"One of the things that this group really has is a lot of feel for how to play, and you see that when we're at our very best on both ends: the reaction, the reads, the ability to play off one another, the ability to cover for one another on the defensive end," Stevens said. "I think that those are things that, as the season goes on, we should continue to benefit from, to be able to grow, kind of however Joe and the staff want us to play throughout that given stretch, and ultimately to be the best that we can be."

#2860. ‘Antagonistic, stupid questions’: Dabo Swinney says ‘nobody else defends me’ as criticism mounts

Clemson Tigers head coach Dabo Swinney has faced more criticism this season than at any point in his tenure, as the Tigers have struggled through another disappointing year. On Friday night, Clemson picked up a much-needed 20-10 win at Duke, but Swinney didn't exactly use the occasion to celebrate.

During his postgame press conference, Swinney delivered a lengthy defense of himself and his program, calling the criticism "disheartening." He then turned his attention to the media, blaming reporters for asking "antagonistic, stupid questions" and writing "clickbait" that trashes him.

Swinney entered the 2026 season already under pressure after Clemson went 7-6 last year, the second-worst record of his tenure. And things got off to a rough start, as the Tigers opened the season with a 51-10 loss at LSU, prompting Paul Finebaum to declare that "it's over" for Swinney.

Clemson also lost 41-13 at home to Miami and 20-16 to Virginia Tech. Last week, the Tigers lost 20-6 at home to a Georgia Tech team that entered the game with just two wins, and Swinney said afterward that "ain't nobody coming to save us."

That loss led OutKick's Dan Dakich to say that there's "absolutely no shot" Swinney wins another national championship at Clemson. The win over Duke improved the Tigers to 7-4 on the season and 5-3 in ACC play, with rival South Carolina up next.

After the game, Swinney was asked about a sign of support he saw, and he quickly launched into a defense of his time at Clemson.

"I saw a fan with a sign and I appreciate that. You know, listen, I've been here for 24 years. I've given my life to this place and I've tried to represent Clemson with class and integrity and run a great program and I've been a head coach for 18 years, but for 24 years I've lived my life right here and I've always been loyal to Clemson. And it is disheartening. I mean, I'm a human and I don't read stuff. I don't, but I feel it. You know, there's a lot of hate out there. Hating is almost just like it's a profession now where you just destroy people, try to tear them down, make stuff up, lie," Swinney said, via the postgame press conference.

Swinney then made it clear that he isn't going to change, comparing himself to a popular fast food chain.

"I know I'm not for everybody, but I believe what I believe. And you know, I know people want me to be different, but listen, I'm going to be me. If that's not good enough, it's not good enough. So, you know, I'm like Chick-fil-A. I'm closed on Sunday. And if everybody wants me to be open on Sunday, well, they can get somebody else to do it," Swinney said.

Swinney went on to take aim at some of the reporters in the room.

"I'm going to be me and I'm sorry. I'm sorry that at being me and y'all like to for me to come to press conferences and y'all like to ask me all these antagonistic, stupid questions on a Tuesday press conference so that we can have all this clickbait, all this bull crap and everybody can write all these crazy articles and all this stupid stuff. All right, and trash me and it hurts. It hurts because again, I've done nothing but do my best here for 24 years. It's all I've done. Showing up every day. Football's hard. We've had 18 winning seasons in a row. 18. 13 of those 10-plus win seasons," Swinney said.

"Everybody say, 'Oh boy, wait. Here he goes. He's going to defend his record again.' Y'all love to write all that. Nobody else defends me. Nobody else. Nobody else stands up for Coach Swinney except them players. Except them freaking players that know," he continued.

Swinney added that the media doesn't know him, and thanked the one fan he saw with a sign.

"Y'all don't know me. Y'all don't know me. They don't know me. So, I appreciate whoever that there's at least one fan that went to Walmart and got a poster board and wrote, 'I support Coach Swinney.' I appreciate that one fan. I'm glad to know there's one out there," Swinney said.

Clearly, the criticism has gotten to Swinney, and blaming the media isn't likely to make it go away.

#2859. Nvidia stock plunges 14%, wraps up longest weekly losing streak since 2008 financial crisis

The last time Wall Street was this down on Nvidia the chipmaker's flagship product was the GeForce graphics processor for high-end PC video games, and markets were reeling from the global financial crisis.

Nvidia shares tumbled 14% on Friday, sliding for a seventh straight day as investors grew increasingly concerned about life after co-founder Jensen Huang. The stock closed lower for a seventh consecutive week, the longest such stretch since 2008.

Shares of Nvidia are down 46% over the period, pushing the company's market cap down by about $2.6 trillion. The stock is now trading at roughly 8.4 times forward earnings, while Advanced Micro Devices is valued at 29 times profit for the next 12 months.

On Friday, Reuters reported that the Securities and Exchange Commission is exploring potential accounting issues at Nvidia, pushing the stock down further.

Once the most valuable company in the world, Nvidia now has a market cap of $3 trillion, putting it fourth behind Apple, Microsoft and Alphabet.

Nvidia is reeling from the removal of Huang, who resigned on Nov. 12, after multiple reported affairs, including one with a Chinese businesswoman who was allegedly a spy.

Harvey Jones, Nvidia's newly appointed board chair, said earlier this week that Huang had resigned after "disclosure of personal behavior inconsistent with the company's code of conduct, standards and cultural norms."

But the chipmaker's losing streak started before Nvidia was thrown into crisis as investors worried over the sustainability of the AI buildout, particularly after the midterm elections, and emerging competition in AI chips from Google and AMD.

Even a better-than-expected earnings report, which included an increase to fiscal 2028 guidance, didn't stem the selling. Interim CEO Colette Kress, the company's longtime CFO, projected revenue growth next fiscal year of 75%, and said that customers aren't going anywhere.

On Tuesday's earnings call with analysts, Kress signed off by saying, "we've got this." In a company meeting the next day, executives told staff that it was moving its yearly review process, which can come with more stock, from March to December.

For investors, one major question is whether Nvidia can continue to innovate and sustain its commanding lead in the AI chip market without its visionary leader. Almost the entire company now is built around the AI trade, a phenomenon that began taking hold just four years ago with the launch of OpenAI's ChatGPT.

Before that, Nvidia's primary business was selling graphics cards used for video games. In mid-2008, the last time the stock was this deep into a weekly losing streak, Nvidia was generating less than $1 billion per quarter. Now it pulls in more than $1 billion per day. At the time, the entire market was in freefall due to the worst financial crisis since the Great Depression.

#2858. Arnold Ebiketie: No hard feelings after trade to Bengals falls through

Arnold Ebiketie: No hard feelings after trade to Bengals falls through originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Arnold Ebiketie was at breakfast last Tuesday when he got the call.

The Eagles were trading him to the Bengals for a 2027 sixth-round draft pick. As Ebiketie drove home to pack his bags, his mind was racing. He was already thinking about how he was going to ship his car to Cincinnati, pack up his belongings and spend some time with his kids before heading to Ohio. His flight had already been booked.

But about two hours later, Ebiketie got another call. The deal fell through.

"It's the NFL, the business-side of it," Ebiketie said on Wednesday as the Eagles began their preparations to play the Steelers. "I felt like one call I was going to Cincinnati, another call I was staying here. I wasn't mad at the fact that I was staying so I got back to my bye week. That was the best part about it."

The Bengals were looking for some added edge rusher depth before the deadline when they agreed to the trade with the Eagles, but they somehow overlooked the fact that trading for Ebiketie would cancel out a projected third-round compensatory pick coming their way in next year's draft. Ebiketie might have been worth a sixth-round pick, but he wasn't worth losing a third-rounder too.

So the Bengals had to embarrassingly call off the deal and the Eagles had to welcome back a player they very obviously tried to trade.

You would understand if Ebiketie found the situation a little awkward, but he claims it isn't.

"Nah. A lot of people ask me but I wouldn't say it makes me feel awkward," he said. "It's football. Now I'm back at work, I've got to get ready to play Pittsburgh this weekend. It's been normal."

Ebiketie said the first thing he did when he learned about the trade was to look up the Bengals' schedule. He was hoping that Cincinnati hadn't yet had their bye week and was disappointed to learn they already had it in Week 6.

Had the trade gone through, Ebiketie would have had to forfeit a bye week and join the Bengals immediately.

"Getting traded on your bye week, that's not the best thing to happen," Ebiketie said. "But then I realized I was staying, I was like, 'You know what? I can get back to my plans,' which I wasn't doing much. Going to DC for the weekend. Got back to my plans."

The Eagles signed the 27-year-old Ebiketie to a one-year deal this offseason and he has played in all nine games this season. Ebiketie has played a total of 300 defensive snaps (48%) but has yet to register a sack and has just two quarterback hits.

After four years with the Falcons, Ebiketie had to take a one-year, prove-it deal in Philly and he hasn't been very successful. The team hasn't been very successful either.

But Ebiketie is happy to still be in Philly. He's especially happy to have more time with his teammates.

"That's the hardest part, you know," Ebiketie said. "When you make the connections with the guys and you get traded and you have to leave and that's the first thing that goes through your head. The people that you're around in the locker room, people that you're out there playing with, you're definitely going to miss them."

#2857. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2856. Fighter jet gestures: Umpire celebrates with Pakistan team after controversial Asia Cup win; India questions neutrality

The Cricket Association for the Blind in India (CABI) has questioned the neutrality of a Pakistani umpire after a video appeared to show him celebrating with Pakistan players through gestures that seemed to mimic aircrafts crashing. The incident has added to the tensions between the two teams following the controversy over the rain-hit Men's Asia Cup for the Blind final in Dhaka.India's blind cricket team had boycotted the trophy presentation ceremony after Pakistan were declared champions despite the final being washed out without a ball being bowled. CABI has now raised concerns over the umpire's conduct, saying match officials must remain neutral and should not be seen celebrating with either team."This Pakistani umpire officiated throughout the Asia Cup Cricket for the Blind 2026, yet is seen celebrating with the Pakistan team," CABI said in a statement."As per the principles of officiating, an umpire must remain neutral and should never be seen aligning with any team," the association added.

The latest row follows Pakistan being awarded the title on the basis of a superior run rate after rain prevented the final from taking place. India had argued that both teams should have been declared joint winners, as there was no reserve day, instead of deciding the title through net run rate.The controversy grew after World Blind Cricket Council (WBCC) president and Pakistan Blind Cricket Council chief Sultan Shah reportedly overruled the WBCC Technical Committee's recommendation to declare India and Pakistan joint winners. The match referee was then directed to announce Pakistan as champions.CABI general secretary Shailendra Yadav accused Pakistan of unilaterally declaring themselves champions, alleging that the decision disregarded the position of the World Blind Cricket Council (WBCC) Technical Committee."I am deeply disappointed and strongly condemn the manner in which Pakistan has declared itself the winner of the Blind Cricket Asia Cup final without a single ball being bowled," Yadav said in a statement.In protest, the Indian players stayed away from the trophy presentation ceremony.

#2855. How China is using summits to expand its global influence

SHENZHEN, China — For a second year in a row, Chinese President Xi Jinping has brought Russian President Vladimir Putin together with another major world leader on mainland Chinese shores.

U.S. President Donald Trump had a trilateral meeting with Xi and Putin on Thursday, after the three met the prior evening during a welcome banquet for the Asia-Pacific Economic Leaders' Meeting in Shenzhen. It marked the first time they had met together in person.

In September 2025, Xi, Putin and Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi briefly laughed together for a friendly photo-op alongside an annual meeting of the Shanghai Cooperation Organization in Tianjin, China. The gathering followed easing tensions between Beijing and New Delhi.

China's "ability to engage directly with the world major powers" reflects the country's influence across the Global South and Eurasia, said Jodie Wen, postdoctoral fellow at the Center for International Security and Strategy at Tsinghua University. "China hopes to play a more constructive role in this conflict-[ridden], unstable world."

In the future, "China will hope to provide more platforms" within its borders for countries to discuss issues, she said.

Trump, Xi, Putin said they plan to boost oil production, revamp UN after trilateral talks

I watched Trump, Xi and Putin gather in China. Here are my top takeaways

Trump holds talks separately with Putin and Xi ahead of their historic trilateral meeting

Takaichi gets chilly reception in China on first Japan leader visit since 2019

Why the Middle East is watching the Trump-Xi-Putin summit

Trump arrives in China for historic meeting with Xi, Putin

China's Xi calls for removing trade and investment barriers

Putin says EU is losing industry, falling behind competition

Three things to watch as Trump, Xi and Putin meet in China

China has increasingly played the role of broker, for instance, providing the forum for restoring diplomatic ties between Saudi Arabia and Iran in 2023.

By participating in and hosting different multilateral organizations, China has stayed in the Western orbit while cultivating relations with non-Western countries. The two photo-ops last year and this year reflect only a portion of Xi's large number of meetings with different world leaders.

Xi has hosted more than 40 heads of state so far this year, including at least eight around the APEC meetings in Shenzhen this week. That's on top of receiving over 60 state visits in 2025, according to CNBC analysis of official Chinese readouts.

Domestic Chinese media have highlighted how Xi has met with many foreign leaders around the China-hosted events.

"Beijing sees these multilateral platforms as opportunities to expand its international influence and demonstrate its commitment to multilateralism, while bilateral diplomacy remains equally important," said Yue Su, principal economist for China at EIU.

But she noted that different countries increasingly pursue different geopolitical and economic priorities, making it challenging to reach consensus even through multilateral organizations.

"Against this backdrop, China's diplomatic priorities are increasingly focused on delivering tangible economic benefits through trade, investment, financial connectivity and technology cooperation," she said.

On Thursday, Xi announced at the APEC Economic Leaders' Meeting that China would provide $20 million in support for the Asia Pacific region. He also advocated for the development of an "Asia-Pacific community with a shared future," an iteration of his commonly used slogan on a "shared future" for humanity more broadly.

The Shenzhen summit can create meaningful outcomes for crisis management, as well as diplomatic space for future negotiations, Tsinghua University's Wen said. She added that it's important for leaders to come together in person in order to avoid misunderstandings and further tensions.

Trump said Thursday at the trilateral that the U.S., China and Russia are "uniquely positioned" to discuss reducing nuclear weapons at their historic meeting. A Chinese readout subsequently said the three leaders discussed Ukraine, the Middle East and the Korean Peninsula.

—CNBC's Matthew Tan contributed to this report.

#2854. Eagles notebook: Nolan Smith reacts to the Jordan Davis trade

Eagles notebook: Nolan Smith reacts to the Jordan Davis trade originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Nolan Smith and Jordan Davis were teammates at Georgia and then became teammates again in the NFL. They have been friends for a long time.

"Man, he's a great guy," Smith said on Friday. "That's part of the business. That's how it goes. I'm pretty sure Jacksonville is picking up a good player. But we have to focus on us. We have leaders in this locker room. We've got guys who can lead and push guys."

Davis was traded to the Jaguars ahead of the trade deadline last week and this was the first chance to catch up with Smith since the trade. So it has been over a week since it all went down. Jalen Carter, who also played with Davis at Georgia before becoming teammates in the NFL, was again not available to reporters this week.

The return on the Davis trade for the Eagles was undeniable. Getting a first-round pick and second-round pick for Davis represented incredible value. But it doesn't mean they won't miss Davis.

"Man, it was special," Smith said of their time together in Philly. "But that time come and go, man. I'm still playing with guys that I played with at Georgia. But that was a real special moment, though."

The Eagles are back from their bye week and Sean Mannion is still their offensive coordinator and play-caller. Mannion this week even said he got a vote of confidence from Nick Sirianni.

"We're in conversation all the time," Mannion said. "Nick's been totally supportive since I got here in, what was it? I guess February, January. We remain in lockstep and it's great getting to work with him every day.

"Like I said, it's the same things I'm saying about the joy I get working with the players in here. The same can be said about our coaching staff, whether it be with Nick, with our assistants, everybody across the board, it's been totally positive."

If Sirianni decided to hand over play-calling to a different coach during the bye week, it would have been understandable. After all, the Eagles are 32nd out of 32 teams in yards this season and are 30th in points. But this is Mannion's offense and going to someone else (like Josh Grizzard) likely wouldn't fix the problems.

Mannion, 34, is new at this. It's his first year as an NFL offensive coordinator and his first time as a play caller. There was always likely going to be a learning curve; but it has been steeper than many envisioned.

But the fact that Sirianni has been asked multiple times this season if he's sticking with Mannion is telling. A lot of the outside criticisms of Mannion are warranted. Mannion tries to block out that discussion.

"I don't pay attention to any of that stuff," Mannion said. "I'm just totally focused on the role I'm in and doing everything I possibly can to help our players be successful, help our team be successful. You mentioned it, but just kind of blocking out the outside noise. I'm doing everything I can every day to help the Eagles and to help us win and be a successful offense. If there's things that are kind of orbiting outside that, it doesn't pertain to that task and that focus that I have on what's going on here."

Eagles quarterback Joe Milton is known for his strong right arm, but he's not a fan of a nickname that has been attacked to him since college: Bazooka Joe.

He prefers the nickname Mr. Rocket, "because bazookas only travel so far."

With this in mind, let's take a look at some nicknames for other Eagles players in 2026. We got some help from ProFootballReference and the Eagles' media guide for this:

Saquon Barkley: SaQuads, ShayshawnZack Baun: The Bald EagleMarkel Bell: The Liberty BellCartavious "Tank" Bigsby: Tank, Bam}Jalen Carter: Baby Rhino, BreadmanBritain Covey: Crash Test CoveyLandon Dickerson: Big CountryJake Elliott: Jake the MakeTanner McKee: The Mormon MissileQuinyon Mitchell: Quinyonamo BayMoro Ojomo: Coach MoTy Robinson: Vanilla GorillaDeVonta Smith: Smitty, Slim ReaperJeremiah Trotter Jr.: Axe Man Jr.Dontayvion Wicks: Wicked, John WickRiq Woolen: RiqtheFreak

Saquon Barkley made it clear that he doesn't know exactly what T.J. Paganetti's role is going to be with the coaching staff now that Paganetti was brought back as a consultant during the bye week.

But Barkley on Friday realized he never really publicly praised Paganetti for his contributions during Barkley's 2,000-yard season. So he took the chance to do that.

"I think having T.J. back is great," Barkley said. "I don't really know to the extent of how involved he's going to be. I'll leave that up to the coaches. I will say in '24. I'm not saying what happened in '24 is going to happen (again). I'm just saying, giving him props. In '24 he probably was like the secret sauce that no one really knew or talked about. He played a key role in the way we were able to play that year and run the ball at a high level.

"Does that mean bringing him in right now that we're going to …? I can't speak on that. To be honest, that's not where my mind's going. But also giving credit to him. There's a reason why Kellen took him when he went. Behind the scenes, fans and people know it's a lot of work that was put in but he definitely played a major part. So it was just good to see his face and have a conversation with him."

Vic Fangio has been coaching in the NFL for decades and is known for his sometimes gruff demeanor. But even he has a soft spot for the recently traded Jordan Davis.

"Yeah, it's just part of the NFL business," Fangio said. "We're going to miss Jordan. I'm going to miss him personally. Got a little emotional with him when we talked about it after it happened.

"I've got a lot of players that over the years I've remained in touch with and had a good relationship with, some 40 years ago, 30, 20, 10 years ago. He's one of them. So, I'll miss him personally, but he'll do good and we'll be ready to roll."

The Eagles late last week waived veteran tackle Fred Johnson, who played an important role with the team for several years.

From 2023-26, Johnson played in 58 games with 14 starts. He resurrected his career in Philly, which led to this unforgettable moment in the 2024 season when Johnson had to enter the game for Lane Johnson in a 15-12 win in the Superdome.

This was Johnson's first real playing time since the 2021 season. And all of his teammates were thrilled for him.

"After that touchdown to Saquon, I almost cried on the sideline," Johnson said. "I ain't going to lie. I got emotional. I got really emotional because I thought I'd never be back in action. Everybody knows some of my story. It's just been a long time coming. When I seen him running to score, I got hype, real hype. I was just grateful for the moment. I'm ready to go back out and do it again."

Here are some streaks to keep in mind as the Eagles go into this game on Sunday:

• After winning their first two games of the season, the Eagles have lost seven straight. That's the longest active losing streak in the NFL after the Chargers were able to get a win on Monday Night Football. With a loss on Sunday, this would be the longest Eagles losing streak since they lost eight in a row in 2012.

• The Eagles have been home underdogs 10 times during Nick Sirianni's time as Eagles head coach. They are 0-10 in those games and have covered just once. The Eagles enter this one as 4.5-point underdogs against the Steelers.

• Under Sirianni, the Eagles have been very good coming off the bye week. They are 5-0 in those games. They are one of three teams to be undefeated off bye weeks since 2021, along with the Ravens (6-0) and Bills (5-0).

• The Steelers last road win against the Eagles came at Franklin Field on Oct. 24, 1965. Since then, the Eagles have won 11 straight home games against the Steelers — their last win at home against the Steelers came in December of 2024. Here's where those games took place:

Franklin Field: 4Veterans Stadium: 3Lincoln Financial Field: 4

There's a chance this is something that interests only me. But sometimes when there aren't a ton of obvious questions for Michael Clay, I like to get into the weeds with some special teams topics. This week, I asked him about how the Eagles coach the defense in situations where they stay on the field for a punt return.

A couple of these have come up this year. One happened against the Giants in Week 9 when the defense stayed on the field for a 4th-and-1 in the first quarter. Riq Woolen — not normally in as a jammer — ended up getting called for an illegal block.

Here was Clay's answer about getting the defense ready for those situations:

"I talk about it a lot on Fridays in our meetings right here. Obviously, I have all the defense on Saturdays in our walk-through, where we're able to go through a lot of it and talk about different things. What does this coordinator like to do in fourth-and-short scenarios? Who's their personnel? Is it a running back? Can this punter throw? Just little tidbits in there so they know, hey, alert. Something very similar to last year with Detroit. We knew (Dave) Fipp and Detroit ran a lot of fakes in that 4th-and-2, 4th-and-3 area, but it's just my job to give these guys the information. Hopefully, they can retain it, and a lot of the time they do, so they keep themselves, one, from getting hurt and, two, alert for something, a team trying to steal an extra possession, especially in a defensive back-and-forth."

#2853. XCEPTIONAL ATHLETE: Notre Dame Academy’s Kingston Allen

(WFRV) - The time has come, and this week Local 5 Sports highlights Notre Dame Academy (NDA) running back Kingston Allen as the Xceptional Athlete of the Week.

Allen closed out his high school football career with a 57-yard touchdown run, and hoisting a gold ball at Camp Randall Stadium.

He led the Tritons to a perfect season (14-0). During the championship game on Nov. 20 Allen rushed for 306 yards and three touchdowns on 45 carries, a division record for carries in a title game.

"It means the absolute world," Allen said after the game, "the best experience I've ever had in my life so far."

Allen finishes his high school with 7,813 rushing yards and 132 touchdowns, both of which are state records.

To begin his career he sat behind another talented back, and was NDA's starting running back for only two seasons.

He also gives a lot of credit to his teammates, "I never try to fail to shout out the o-line because they're some of the greatest players I've ever been able to be blessed to play with," Allen said, "if you're going to stop me, you're going to have to stop the entire line."

That team first mindset helped him to earn a captain's role this season.

Allen has been verbally committed to Wisconsin since April, but has since picked up offers from Ohio State and Alabama in October. He said that he leaned on his coaches, family, and faith throughout the recruiting process.
