#2852. Fresno State holds off a late rally to beat Colorado State 49-42 and become bowl eligible

FRESNO, Calif. (AP) — Jayden Mandal threw for three touchdowns, Rayshon Luke had two rushing touchdowns and a receiving score, and Fresno State held off a late rally to beat Colorado State 49-42 on Saturday night.

Luke's second rushing touchdown, set up by Josiah Freeman's 60-yard catch-and-run, made it 49-27 with 13:57 left in the fourth.

It was a 22-point game until Colorado State scored two touchdowns in the final two minutes, aided by a Fresno State fumble, and the Rams converted the two-point conversion on a trick play for a one-possession deficit. The ensuing onside kick went off a Fresno State player and Colorado State recovered it with 25 seconds left.

Colorado State did not have any timeouts left and Hauss Hejny scrambled for nine yards to get to Fresno State's 36, but the clock wound down to nine seconds. After a completion put the Rams at the 28 with four seconds left, Hejny scrambled to his left for a pass into the endzone that fell incomplete.

Fresno State (6-5) became bowl eligible for the sixth straight season. The Bulldogs have won four straight at home, tying the number of wins at home in 2024 and '25.

Mandal was 15-of-17 passing for 191 yards. Freeman finished with five catches for 104 yards and two touchdowns.

Hejny threw for 413 yards and two touchdowns with an interception for Colorado State (3-8).

Mandal, who needed to be helped off the field with 44 seconds left in the first half, started the second half and handed it off to Bryson Donelson on the first two plays — with the second going for a 61-yard touchdown. Fresno State needed just four plays to score on its next possession — a 21-yard connection between Mandal and Freeman to make it 42-20 midway through the third.

Colorado State and Fresno State await opponents for flex week.

#2850. Victor Wembanyama's historic masterclass continues to strengthen his case for the MVP

Victor Wembanyama's historic masterclass continues to strengthen his case for the MVP originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

On the MVP ladder, Victor Wembanyama is joined by Luka Doncic at the top of the list. At this point, it is hard to decide between the two players because they have been stellar for the San Antonio Spurs and the Los Angeles Lakers, respectively.

Both players have had many impressive nights, but Wembanyama had another one on Saturday against the New Orleans Pelicans. He posted 50 points, 19 rebounds, four steals, and five blocks to lead the Spurs to victory.

Wembanyama is only in his fourth NBA season, but he has already proven himself as a top star. As a defender, he has arguably been the best in the league since he joined the NBA in 2023.

He finally won a Defensive Player of the Year award in the 2025-26 season, which was also the campaign that saw the Spurs make the NBA Finals after reaching the Playoffs for the first time in nearly a decade.

MORE: Victor Wembanyama has created a new shot that has amazed the NBA world

Apart from his defensive attributes, Wembanyama has also cemented himself as one of the most dominant offensive players. This has been clear this season because he already had a 52-point game, but on Saturday, he got a 50-point performance against New Orleans.

"Growing up, I was almost always the youngest player, the rookie, or playing up an age group. So taking 29 shots was unthinkable, and that's just the natural progression. It was also one of the challenges for me when I first arrived here," Wembanyama said regarding his growth in the NBA.

This has been proof that Wembanyama has reached a level of bravery that he did not have in the past. Now, he is not a player who is developing because he has cemented himself as a top player.

The performance against the Pelicans was a historic night because he is the first player since the 1973-74 season to record those types of numbers, but if it was up to Wembanyama, the win is the most important part.

#2849. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

#2848. Warren Buffett quadruples Thanksgiving gifts: A breakdown of how much wealth he's giving away

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Warren Buffett, 96, more than quadrupled his Thanksgiving donations to four family foundations this year to $5.5 billion.

The gifts consist of Berkshire Class B shares that have been converted from his roughly $160 billion of Class A shares.

The increase is in line with his new goal, announced last July, to give away all of his Berkshire Hathaway shares "within about eight years," with no additional annual donations going to the Gates Foundation.

Around $2.2 billion of shares goes to the Susan Thompson Buffett Foundation, named for his late first wife, with the three foundations run by his children, Susie Buffett's Sherwood Foundation, the Howard G. Buffett Foundation, and Peter Buffett's NoVo Foundation each getting $1.1 billion each.

In July of last year, Buffett gave shares worth $4.6 billion to the Gates Foundation with another $1.4 billion going to the family foundations.

This July, the family foundations received $5.9 billion in stock, bringing the year's total to $11.4 billion, an increase of almost 55% from last year, even as the Gates Foundation received nothing.

In comments included in the Berkshire news release this week announcing the new gifts, Buffett reaffirmed his confidence in his children's ability to disburse his fortune over time, saying they "have given thoughtfully to worthy causes for decades, and I trust 100% both their abilities and their fidelity."

He also said his confidence in Berkshire is "as strong as ever."

Recalling that in last year's lengthier Thanksgiving message he had said, "Kindness is costless but also priceless," Buffett wrote, " A year later, I would only add that it compounds, and at a better rate than anything Charlie [Munger] and I ever bought."

"Both of us received beyond measurement costless benefits from a few teachers such as Ben Graham, Joe Rosenfield and Tom Murphy. Because of this we not only prospered mightily but were able to pass along many of the benefits we received.

"Much of my good fortune has come in the form of people who were kind to me and expected nothing in return. I'm grateful to every one of them.

"This Thanksgiving, thank one of those selfless people in your own life. Better yet, be a benefactor for someone else."

Berkshire Hathaway's fourth quarter rally that had produced a string of record highs cooled this week as both classes of its stock posted five straight daily declines.

Last Friday, Berkshire was up 19.1% since October 1. Now that gain has been cut to 14.5%

The S&P's decline of 8.6% this week, however, was even bigger than Berkshire's 5-day drop, as investors sold to lock in profits from AI-related stocks.

The benchmark index, along with the Dow Industrials, are in a correction after falling 10% from their recent highs, and the Nasdaq Composite has erased all of its year-to-date gains.

Berkshire's smaller decline increased its year-to-date outperformance of the S&P to 12.5 percentage points.

One Wall Street firm, however, thinks Berkshire's run-up has run its course.

Argus Research downgraded the B shares to hold from buy Friday, saying the stock is "fully valued" at about 25 times next year's estimate for operating earnings per share.

As foreshadowed by its 10-Q financial report released almost two weeks ago, Berkshire Hathaway's Q3 13F portfolio snapshot filed with the SEC on Monday showed it wasn't doing much buying and selling of equities this summer.

And the two largest additions, Lennar and Bending Spoons, had been previously disclosed, along with additional purchases at the start of the fourth quarter.

The most notable change we didn't expect was a 12% increase in Berkshire's Delta Air Lines position, bringing it just short of a 10% stake, now valued at $5.2 billion.

Exceeding 10% triggers a number of regulatory burdens, including the requirement to promptly disclose new buying or selling, so we may not see additional purchases going forward.

Delta's return to the portfolio after Warren Buffett sold all of Berkshire's airline stocks in 2020 has been something of a surprise given his bumpy ride with the industry over the years.

As expected, Berkshire continued to trim its Bank of America stake. After nine consecutive quarters of sales, it's been cut by 56% but remains the fifth largest portfolio holding at $21.4 billion.

Berkshire also cut another financial, Capital One, by almost 62%, although it was already a relatively small position, now worth $207 million.

Also notable are two positions that didn't change.

After adding $17 billion in Q2 to the Alphabet bet that had only been initiated last summer, Berkshire defied some expectations by keeping the holding unchanged.

Its 106 million shares are valued at $28.1 billion.

And for the fourth consecutive quarter, Berkshire's largest equity holding, Apple, didn't change. With a big boost from the stock's year-to-date gain of 26.1%, the position is now valued at more than $78 billion.

Warren Buffett loves to eat what many would call junk food and drink a lot of sugary beverages. A quarter-century ago, when he was 70, Buffett explained why he still thought a life insurance company would rate his life expectancy "considerably better than the average."

AUDIENCE MEMBER: Every time I hear what you like to eat, Warren, it makes me wonder what your cholesterol level is — (laughter) — or if you even worry about it...

WARREN BUFFETT: I do know the number, and I don't remember it. My doctor tells me, "It's a little high," but if he says it's a little high, it means it isn't that high, or he would — because he always tries to push me into making a few changes in my life.

But he — I've got a wonderful doctor. And I was lucky last year, because I hadn't been in to see him for about five years. And — (laughter) — due to — those guys cost a lot of money, I mean. (Laughter)

And due to purely an accident, a reaction to some other medicine I was given when I was out of the city, he got a hold of me, and then he shamed me into having a physical. And it was extremely lucky, because I had a polyp in the colon that would have probably caused trouble, you know, within a couple of years.

I would say that if you ask my doctor, he would want me to make a few changes, but he would also say that my life expectancy is probably a lot better than the average person of 70.

You know, I have no stress whatsoever. Zero. You know, I mean, I get to do what I love to do every day. You know, and I'm surrounded by people that are terrific.

So that problem in life just doesn't exist for me. You know, and I don't smoke or drink or, well, we'll end it right there. (Laughter)

And so, you know, if you were an underwriter for a life company, you would rate me considerably better than the average. You'd rate Charlie better than average, too.

And I'm sure that, you know, I could change it slightly, perhaps, on the probabilities, you know, if I change my diet dramatically or something. But it's very unlikely to happen.

#2847. Sixers get lucky with their 14th win as Herro can’t get off game-tying three in time

The Sixers just outlasted the Milwaukee Bucks by a literal second, hanging on to win 132-129 Saturday afternoon, notching their fifth win in a row and improving to 14-3 on the season.

Joel Embiid did a lot to make up for a subpar shooting night, finishing with a team-high 26 points, a season-high 17 rebounds, and nine assists shooting 7-of-17 from the floor. After missing last game with a foot sprain, Tyrese Maxey had one of his quietest games of the year finishing with 17 points on 4-of-11 shooting.

LeBron James played quarterback for much of the day, finishing with 21 points, six rebounds and six assists going 9-of-14. VJ Edgecombe shot 5-of-12 from the floor to finish with 16 points with three rebounds and six assists. Tyler Herro led all scorers with 50 but his three to tie the game just didn't beat the buzzer.

Jaylen Brown and Dean Wade missed their second straight game, both dealing with hamstring strains, along with Adem Bona, who's missed the last eight. Milwaukee was without Ousmane Dieng.

The Sixers got two of their easiest buckets all season to open the game off of cuts. James went down the baseline and Edgecombe flashed to the middle of the paint. Their pull-up jump shooting was a different story early. Embiid even had a midrange blocked by Myles Turner as the Sixers struggled to capitalize on a 1-of-8 start for the Bucks.

Embiid also had a layup attempt blocked by Turner as he started the game 0-of-4 from the field. His other two jumpers falling just off the rim. On the other end the Sixers allowed two offensive rebounds to turn into second-chance points as the Bucks' offense was the first to find a rhythm.

The Sixers' offense settled in after an early timeout from Nick Nurse. They got a couple of open threes from Anfernee Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Edgecombe broke Turner down off the dribble, and LeBron opened up plenty of space on a drive to hit Embiid with a lob. James made a great one-handed pass cross court to find KCP in the corner a few possessions later but the three rimmed out. KCP and Embiid also both missed fast break layups that halted a run.

Jabari Walker checked in next to Embiid at the four like he has in recent games and was rewarded for cutting by Embiid shortly after checking in. Dominick Barlow made his season debut a few minutes later, relieving Embiid. Labaron Philon Jr. remained in the rotation and picked up a floater, but also fouled Herro attempting a three right before the quarter ended. Herro made all three to give the Bucks a one-point lead after one.

Barlow's first big impact was tipping back a missed Maxey floater for Philly's first points of the second. On the next possession he ran down the floor to catch a lob after LeBron had just about singlehandedly created. It wasn't all perfect — the Bucks scored six straight field goals in the paint, but it was a fine first shift back for Barlow.

LeBron was easily the best player on the floor for the Sixers. His ability to lead people open gave them a lot of easy baskets that were much needed for an offense struggling to hit jumpers. They were also struggling to finish off defensive possessions as the Bucks picked up their 10th second-chance point on eight offensive rebounds.

Maxey got downhill quick and drew a foul which gave Nurse a chance to use a timeout. Coming out of that break, it seemed like there was an ever bigger focus to run quickly, whether if it was after a make or a miss. After Maxey zoomed through for a layup, Edgecombe got down the floor twice, setting up Walker and Embiid for buckets at the rim.

It was weird because the Sixers weren't playing great defense by any means, but the Bucks still had a bunch of airballs in the first half, more than any NBA team should. The misses the Sixers got were ugly, but they hadn't made life hard on Herro at all — he was able to get to just about every spot on the floor.

The first trouble Herro ran into all night was when he tried to contest an Embiid dunk in transition and took a shot to the face while unsuccessfully doing so. The dunk seemed to finally get Embiid going as he was able to bully past Turner for an and-1 after trying all half to do so. That capped off an 8-0 run that gave the Sixers a one-point lead at the break.

Sandwiched in between a pair of free throws from Maxey and Embiid, LeBron got to the basket for another big dunk. A few possessoins later, Embiid finally got a midrange to go over Turner. Not much had changed defensively as the Bucks scored on their first four possessions of the second half.

The Sixers definitley got more comfortable in their half court offense as the game went on. After paint buckets from Maxey and Edgecombe, KCP knocked a three to give the Sixers a lead they were able to hang on to for more than one possession. Aside from losing Herro once on the perimeter as they came down the floor, the defense had improved as well. Embiid had started to do a good job of finishing possessions as he passed his season high in rebounds.

Simons and KCP were the only Sixers who had made a three to this point in the game, so it was a good thing they were both feeling it. Simons ripped eight straight points to give the Sixers their first double-digit lead of the night.

Embiid threw a nice one-handed pass into the corner for Edgecombe who finally became the third Sixer of the night to hit a three. Edgecombe tried to run quickly off a made free throw, but had his layup attempt blocked by Kel'el Ware. Simons got a chance for yet another buzzer-beater, but his midrange attempt didn't fall, neither did Embiid's humorous attempt to get a shot off with 0.2 seconds remaining. The Sixers held a six-point lead after the third.

Barlow did get up a corner three in his first shift and airballed it pretty bad. He got another chance in his second shift and got plenty of arc to bury it this time. LeBron found him from the high-post for a layup on the next possession to prompt an early Bucks timeout.

Shortly after James found Barlow cutting to the basket again and hit him for the layup, drawing a foul in the process. The Sixers lost their grip on Herro coming out of the timeout though, letting him get to the basket a couple of times. The Sixers nearly forced a turnover, but left AJ Green open in the process, who was able to hit a three to cut it back to a one-possession game.

After calling a timeout, the Sixers put the ball in LeBron's hands, who was able to back in his way in for a bucket, putting back his own missed floater. Thanks to another offensive rebound from Barlow on the next possession, Maxey was able to scoot to the basket for two more. James bullied his way for two more baskets, drawing a foul on one, to restore order a bit.

Milwaukee was able to respond with a basket nearly every time the Sixers scored a big one, but they couldn't get stops with LeBron locked in. They ran the same play just about every time down the floor, having Maxey come up to screen for LeBron while Embiid had the ball, and it got LeBron open every time.

Herro missed an open floater down six with a minute, which seemed like it would put the Bucks away for good. Not only did Embiid come up empty on both Sixers possessions, but the Bucks scored quick off those misses, cutting it back down to two with 12 seconds left. The Sixers hurried to beat the Bucks down the floor and Turner had to foul Embiid, picking up his sixth of the game. Embiid buried both free throws, but Brayden Burries stepped back into the corner, burying the open three to cut it to one with 4.8 to go.

Embiid went way into the backcourt on the inbound to take three seconds off the clock before being fouled. It was hard to tell from press row, but he definitely stepped out of bounds running towards the sideline and got lucky the refs missed it. He made both free throws to make it a three-point game with 1.5 seconds left and the Bucks had one timeout left to advance. The Sixers put Barlow in for Embiid. The Bucks sent Herro down the other end of the court who took a dribble before getting his three up. He buried the shot, silencing the crowd, but replay showed that he just didn't get it off in time, making the Sixers very lucky to escape this one in regulation.

#2846. ‘Isn’t Even Better Than Kyrie Irving,’ ‘Trying To Keep His Job’ — NBA Fans Troll Kenny Atkinson Over James Harden Top-10 Claim

James Harden turned back the clock on Friday night, pouring in 51 points to lift the Cleveland Cavaliers past the Indiana Pacers 128-119 at Rocket Arena. After watching the 37-year-old guard pass Kobe Bryant for the third-most 50-point games in NBA history, head coach Kenny Atkinson took his praise to an unexpected level.

Atkinson made a strong case for Harden's place among the NBA's all-time greats during his postgame press conference, a comment that quickly drew mockery across social media.

Cleveland led 62-61 at halftime, with Harden already at 24 points, before erupting for 43 in the third quarter. Harden scored 18 of them, including 13 during a 21-4 run that helped the Cavs build a 105-83 lead heading into the fourth. Indiana cut the deficit to six late, but Harden scored 7 of Cleveland's final 9 points to close it out.

He finished 15-for-26 from the floor, hit seven 3-pointers and went 14-for-14 at the line, adding 6 rebounds and 6 assists in 40 minutes. It was his highest-scoring game as a Cavalier and his 26th career 50-point outing, trailing only Wilt Chamberlain and Michael Jordan.

Speaking to reporters after the game, Atkinson urged everyone not to take the 18-year veteran for granted.

"Guy's special. We gotta, I know, you know, everyone wants a perfect, but we gotta appreciate this guy," Atkinson said. "You know, like, this guy is top 10 of all time. I know I appreciate every day coaching him. It's an honor. I really mean that. Doesn't happen to a lot of coaches to get to coach a guy like that."

The comment didn't sit well with everyone. One fan summed up the immediate disbelief, posting, "Top what??" along with a reaction meme.

Another wondered if the Cavaliers coach simply misspoke, asking, "Top 10 for guards right? Not like all time in general?"

Others went straight after Harden's comparisons with his peers. One user bluntly wrote, "Isn't even better than Kyrie Irving."

Irving is a nine-time All-Star and an NBA champion, while Harden has the edge in individual accolades, including the 2018 MVP award and a place on the NBA 75th Anniversary Team.

Sports radio host Brendan Tobin joined the jokes, taking a sarcastic jab at Atkinson: "Let's give it up for Coach analytically we're up 3-1, everybody."

Even fans who acknowledged Harden's big night refused to accept the top-10 label without a fight.

"TOP 10 Of all time FOR WHAT CHOKING??? 😂😂😂. (Fairs tho Harden was amazing today)"

Harden's playoff reputation remains a point of contention, particularly after Cleveland's postseason disappointment last year. The Cavaliers were swept 4-0 by the New York Knicks in the Eastern Conference Finals, with Harden committing 84 turnovers across 18 playoff games.

Harden's career résumé certainly carries weight. He has won the 2018 MVP award, claimed three scoring titles, earned 11 All-Star selections and made the NBA 75th Anniversary Team. He also ranks ninth on the all-time scoring list and second in career 3-pointers, behind only Stephen Curry.

SEE ALSO: 'I Didn't Know Steph Curry Was Gonna Be One of the Greatest Shooters in the World' – Dwight Howard Reveals Major 'Regret' From His NBA Career

Harden also entered Friday's game averaging 16.2 ppg on the season, with 7, 11 and 9 points in three of his previous six appearances.

Meanwhile, with Cleveland sitting at 9-8 and Harden averaging 18.4 points and 6.3 assists through 16 games, one viewer suggested Atkinson had an ulterior motive for the over-the-top praise.

Atkinson, the 2025 Coach of the Year, faced speculation about his job security during last season's playoffs.

The Cavaliers will look to build on the win when they visit the Chicago Bulls on Monday.

This article originally appeared on Pro Football Network.

Which is why Udoka expects more from them than he has seen of late.

#2844. State and federal battle over prediction markets regulation heats up after midterms

The turf war between states and the federal government is intensifying after Democrats secured control of Congress and key governorships on midterm elections night.

While it was widely expected Democrats would step up oversight of the emerging, multibillion-dollar industry if they won control of Congress, the actions after the elections have come from primarily Republican-led states.

At the same time, the Commodity Futures Trading Commission — the federal regulator for prediction markets —  has ramped up its oversight of "mention" markets, where traders speculate on what individuals will say during a speech or broadcast. It's a type of event contract the agency has said is susceptible to manipulation.

CNBC first reported on Tuesday that the CFTC placed a hold on the certification of markets related to President Donald Trump's words in mid-November. Kalshi said it was complying with the order, but CEO Tarek Mansour said at an Axios event on Tuesday that the company disagreed with the regulatory decision.

On Thursday, the Senate passed a bipartisan bill that would ban CFTC-registered entities from listing contracts involving terrorism, assassination, war or military operations. The measure also bars contracts that are "reasonably expected" to resolve based on an individual's death.

Utah Attorney General Derek Brown filed 14 criminal charges against Kalshi and its founders, Mansour and Luana Lopes Lara, on Oct. 28, asserting the company violated a state law that prohibits gambling.

Kalshi's founders are expected to appear in person at Utah's Third District Court on Dec. 4, according to court documents.

"This is a crazy overreach; Utah is clearly playing politics. We look forward to putting a stop to this in court, just like we did in Arizona," said Kalshi's spokesperson Dani Lever.

In a separate, civil complaint on Oct. 20, Utah also named institutional market maker Susquehanna International Group as a defendant, along with Kalshi.

Though Arizona Attorney General Kris Mayes filed a criminal complaint against Kalshi in March, a federal judge blocked the case from proceeding in April.

Louisiana's Republican attorney general also filed a complaint to ban multiple exchanges from operating in the state on Nov. 12. A state judge ordered a temporary restraining order against the prediction market companies that same day.

"Who would have thought that these red states, that were reluctant to jump into the litigation arena, ended up having the most boldest and most innovative solutions," said gambling attorney Daniel Wallach, noting that Louisiana and Utah's latest lawsuits were led by Republican attorneys general.

In response, the CFTC sued Louisiana on Nov. 13. Separately, the federal agency filed suit against Arkansas after that state pursued civil enforcement against Kalshi.

The CFTC has sued 12 states as it tries to defend what it views as its exclusive jurisdiction to regulate prediction markets.

Meanwhile, the CFTC may soon add commissioners. President Donald Trump sent bipartisan nominations to the U.S. Senate on Nov. 12 to fill two of the four open seats on the commission. Chairman Michael Selig has been the only member since his nomination was approved by the Senate in December 2025.

Nathan Anonick, a Republican, and Matthew Rowland, a Democrat, appeared before the Senate Agriculture Committee on Tuesday. Multiple senators questioned them on the issue of prediction markets. Both demurred when asked whether they viewed sports-related event contracts as gambling.

The CFTC's position faced another setback in a complicated decision by the Fourth U.S. Circuit Court of Appeals this week. The court ruled 2-1 that Maryland can enforce its state gambling law against Kalshi, though the judges disagreed on the reasoning.

"Judges can't agree with each other on why we're wrong," Kalshi spokesperson Elisabeth Diana said in a statement about the decision. "This splintered decision, including three separate contradictory opinions, demonstrates there is no unified coherent legal argument against prediction markets."

Overall, prediction markets have now seen three losses and one win at the circuit court level. It's widely expected the question of whether states or the federal government can regulate sports-related event contracts will be decided by the Supreme Court.

Kalshi on Nov. 12  ended its advisory relationship with Donald Trump Jr. The prediction platform pointed to his venture capital firm 1789 Capital having a stake in rival platform Polymarket.

Though 1789 Capital announced its holding in Polymarket in August, Kalshi and the president's son ended their relationship after Democrats secured a majority in the House and Senate. Democratic lawmakers are expected to probe companies with relationships to the Trump family.

Nine Democratic senators, led by Adam Schiff, D-California, on Monday sent a letter to the CFTC asking it to reverse a directive to platforms not to geofence their offerings when they receive unfavorable court rulings stopping exchanges from operating in certain states.

Geofencing involves the use of location technology to block users in certain jurisdictions from participating in events contracts.

"Your unilateral decision directly conflicts with active court orders, undermines state and tribal law, and must be reversed," the senators wrote.

Meanwhile, Democratic Rep. Ro Khanna, D-California, has called for Congress to ban sports-related prediction markets entirely.

"Kalshi, a $40B company, lets 18 year olds bet on sports. They call it a 'prediction market.' It's gambling," he wrote in a post on X on Wednesday. "Investigate and ban sports prediction markets."

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

#2843. Knicks’ Mikal Bridges calls game with buzzer-beater 3 vs. Blazers

Knicks' Mikal Bridges calls game with buzzer-beater 3 vs. Blazers appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The New York Knicks nearly let a massive lead slip away against the Portland Trail Blazers on Saturday night, but Mikal Bridges made sure it didn't cost them.

The Knicks forward drilled a 3-pointer at the buzzer to lift New York to a 97-96 win at Madison Square Garden.

The Knicks appeared to be cruising early, pouring in 40 points in the first quarter and building a lead that grew to as many as 26. New York took a 66-43 advantage into halftime, but Portland roared back in the second half, outscoring the Knicks 53-31 over the final two quarters.

The defending champions knocked down 19 3-pointers on the night, yet the offense went cold after the break and allowed the Blazers to chip away at the deficit.

The men in black capped their comeback with a 12-0 run late in the fourth quarter to take a 96-94 lead, stunning the home crowd. With the clock winding down, the ball found Bridges, who calmly knocked down the game-winning triple to send the Garden into a frenzy.

It wasn't the first time Bridges has broken Portland's heart. Back in March 2025, he hit a buzzer-beating 3-pointer in overtime to give the Knicks a 114-113 victory over the Blazers.

With the victory, the Knicks extended their winning streak to four games and improved to 10-6 on the season. It was far from their cleanest performance, but thanks to Bridges, New York escaped with a win it nearly let slip away.

#2840. Tech outages are fueling a new doomsday economy

Americans are shopping like there's no tomorrow.

A spate of high-profile technology outages that grounded planes, halted email access and slowed bank transactions have left many people worried about artificial intelligence-driven cyberattacks and the state of the U.S. energy grid.

Government officials have dismissed rumors and speculation circulating online that there could be a nefarious culprit for the series of malfunctions. Despite those assurances, consumers like Sabrina Cepeda are readying for a larger-scale technology blackout or other emergency event.

In recent days, the Houston resident has purchased propane and lighter fluid for cooking and heating equipment that can be used without electricity. The mother of three also replenished her backup stockpiles of non-perishable foods, hygiene products and allergy medicine in the event that her family is homebound for several days.

"I have a way to keep my family fed, clean, hydrated, warm and safe," said Cepeda, 39. "You want to have these things and not need them, rather than need them and not have them."

The doomsday economy is catching a bid. Wall Street and retailers are taking note.

At least half a dozen major technology outages took place in the U.S. between Nov. 12 and Nov. 17. In that time frame, spending at brands specializing in emergency products spiked about 400% compared with the same period a year prior, according to card data analyzed exclusively for CNBC by Consumer Edge.

At Ready America, which sells products like emergency survival kits, prepackaged water and high-calorie food bars, sales through e-commerce platforms have seen a roughly five-fold increase in recent days. In addition, corporate customers who keep the Vista, California-based company's supplies in their offices in case of emergency have called in to order more.

The company has rarely seen this much demand since the Covid pandemic sent Americans into lockdown more than six years ago, said Ready America President Trevyn Reese. As a result, Ready America asked its suppliers to scale up production. The family-owned business has faced a shortage of water purification tablets, and redirected shoppers to alternative offerings like prepackaged water pouches.

"With everything going on in the news right now, we've seen a large spike of people wanting to get prepared," said Reese.

While the string of outages brought power-related concerns to a boiling point, data shows that worries had been bubbling under the surface for weeks.

Shoppers began significantly ramping up buying from emergency preparedness brands late last month, according to Consumer Edge. Around that time, Fox News reported that the Pentagon was preparing U.S. military bases to operate for at least 90 days without external support. Exacerbating those fears was the ongoing war with Iran that President Donald Trump signaled could ramp back up.

Firearm trade group NSSF said that gun sales rose about 8% in October from the same month a year prior. Retailers and gun manufacturers have anecdotally reported that demand has remained elevated into November, which NSSF said can be attributed to a combination of uncertainty tied to the midterm election and the recent outages.

"If people are investing in firearms and ammunition, they're telling you very directly, with their wallet, what their priority is," said Mark Oliva, NSSF's managing director of public affairs. "You're literally investing in a product that's going to be able to protect your life and protect the lives of your family."

Now, the doomsday talk has begun making its way to Wall Street.

Mizuho told clients on Monday that consumer worries about the outages — coupled with what the bank called "apocalyptic concerns" swirling on social media — drove shoppers to stock up on emergency supplies at Costco. As a result, Mizuho said it expects a key measure of non-fuel sales growth for Costco in November to rise above 10% for the first time since 2022.

And RBC said so-called doomsday prepping has had a "real impact" on buying behavior around the globe over recent weeks. Still, the Canadian bank reported that the trend has so far boosted sales for only a small basket of items, such as shelf-stable proteins, water purification products and dry-pantry foods.

Clients of Richard McWhorter, a private wealth advisor at SRM, have been rushing to Costco to buy goods in bulk and splurging for power generators. McWhorter said he's also fielded questions about how to prepare for a situation in which the financial system goes down.

"I've never seen anything like this in my life," McWhorter said. "There's the financial side of what I do, and then there's the psychological side. And the past two weeks have been much more psychological."

Metal dealer American Hartford Gold reported an uptick in customer inquiries this week. Company president Max Baecker said he heard from some customers that their interest in products like gold — long considered a safe-haven asset during times of turmoil — was driven by newfound concern about being overly reliant on digital financial networks following a glitch at JPMorgan Chase that affected customer transactions for several hours.

Vice President JD Vance abruptly canceled his appearance at a New York Times summit on Wednesday to attend meetings about the technology outages. A day prior, Jay Clayton, the U.S. director of national intelligence, told CNBC that the recent outages were not a cyberattack or a coordinated effort by a foreign nation.

Still, Sen. Mark Warner, D-Va., told CNBC on Wednesday that people were "freaked out" by the outages. Warner, who acts as the vice chairman of the Senate Intelligence Committee, said that the U.S. was "lucky" to have not yet seen a major AI-generated cyberattack or crisis due to AI agents going rogue.

Investors may begin pushing companies to accelerate their cybersecurity spending out of an abundance of caution around rogue AI agents following the outages, according to Macro Hive CEO Bilal Hafeez. Such a situation would be reminiscent of the antivirus software push seen during the dot-com boom, he said.

The Global X Cybersecurity ETF (BUG) has climbed around 3% over the past two weeks, bucking the downtrend seen among AI stocks and the broader technology trade. The Nasdaq Composite on Friday fell into negative territory for 2026, putting the tech-heavy index on track for its first losing year since 2022.

To be sure, not everyone on social media has bought into the idea that they should be prepping for a doomsday scenario. In satirical videos posted on TikTok, users joked that they were gearing up by placing orders in the Starbucks drive-thru or purchasing significant quantities of alcohol.

Still, the topic appears to be on the minds of a growing number of Americans. U.S. Google searches for the term "prepping" have more than doubled when comparing the past month with the same period a year prior. On YouTube, queries for videos about "survivalism," a term describing the practice of preparing for emergencies, have soared by more than 5,000% over the same time frame.

Cepeda, the Houston mom, sees emergency prep efforts as a way to be safe rather than sorry. She's previously tapped into backup food stashes during extreme weather events like Hurricane Harvey in 2017 and Winter Storm Uri in 2021.

But this year is the first time that she's felt she needed to be prepared for a potential emergency caused by technology outages, she said. Cepeda and her husband have ensured they have enough cash on hand and fuel in their cars if there's a broader hit to the U.S. power grid, she said.

"We rely so much on technology," Cepeda said. "You need to be able to do these things the more traditional, old-fashioned way."

#2839. Has Bo Nix quieted early-season criticisms ahead of Las Vegas Raiders-Denver Broncos Week 11 matchup?

Week 11 reignites a longstanding AFC West rivalry between the Las Vegas Raiders and Denver Broncos. To preview the game, Silver and Black Pride reached out to our frenemies at Mile High Report, and Ian St. Clair was kind enough to answer a few questions about the Broncos for Raider Nation.

Q: Bo Nix faced a lot of criticism at the beginning of the season for not pushing the ball down the field. Has that changed over the last handful of games? Is ending last season with a broken ankle impacting how he's been playing in 2026?

A: No matter what happens on the field, Nix is one of those players who will always face criticism. It's also important to add some context for why things may unfold on the field over the course of a game. Early in the season, Nix and the Broncos offense played some of the best defenses in the NFL. On top of that, Denver's running game was horrendous, and that's even putting it mildly.

Opposing defenses knew they could stop the Broncos rushing attack with minimal effort and play two-high safeties. In situations like that, it's going to be hard for a quarterback or offense to push the ball down the field. However, Nix and Denver did open it up a bit more against the Carolina Panthers. Early in the game, Nix took some deep shots that set up the offense for points. We will likely see a continuation of that this Sunday.

As for Nix's injury, there's no doubt it impacted him earlier in the season. I'd say more so mentally than physically. All the physical rehab in the world doesn't rehab the mind until you actually do it and prove to yourself that you can do it. The last few games have been more like the Nix we saw before the injury, and belief and confidence are reemerging.

Q: On a related note, Jaylen Waddle was the Broncos' biggest offseason addition but has just 30 catches for 337 yards and two touchdowns through nine games. What were the expectations for Waddle heading into the season, and what's been holding him back from meeting them?

A: The expectations were high that Waddle would bring an element to Denver's offense that would open it up. Nix and the Broncos would be able to take deep shots and use his speed in the middle of the field. It's been surprising how inconsistent that has been thus far. We've seen flashes, but not nearly enough. Obviously, what I mentioned earlier with the focus opposing defenses can put on the passing game plays into that, but it's also not utilizing him properly. Plus, the fact that Nix has been inconsistent and struggled hasn't helped. Coming out of the bye, I think we'll start to see Waddle play a bigger role in the Broncos offense.

Q: Who would you say is more important to Denver's defense and why: Nik Bonitto or Pat Surtain II?

A: My answer might change in five minutes, but I'll go with Bonitto. Mind you, I think that Surtain is the best cornerback in football. He's one of those cornerbacks who shuts down half the field. However, having a game-changing edge rusher in Bonitto sets the rest of the defense up for success, including Surtain.

What Bonitto brings and how effective he is reminds me of Von Miller, who was the Super Bowl 50 MVP. At any moment, Bonitto can make a play that changes a game. Or, in the case against the Panthers, win it. He's become one of the best edge players in the league, and he just brings so much to the Broncos' defense.

Q: Seahawks head coach/defensive play caller Mike Macdonald gave Fernando Mendoza trouble last week by throwing the book at the rookie with several exotic pressure looks. Should Mendoza expect the same from Vance Joseph this week? What can you tell us about Joseph's scheme that could give a quarterback making his second career start fits?

A: The scheme is only as good as the players who execute it, and that's where I think Mendoza will face similar challenges this Sunday. Denver is one of the best defenses in the NFL, if not the best, at pressuring the quarterback. Even if Joseph doesn't call a blitz, the Broncos front will still make life miserable for Mendoza with Bonitto, Jonathan Cooper, Zack Allen, and Co.

But then there are the times Joseph will send a cornerback or safety on a blitz out of package that Mendoza isn't expecting. But the biggest reason Mendoza will face pressure is the Raiders' currently ineffective running game. Being one-dimensional on offense is never a friend to a rookie quarterback making his second start against a great defense.

Q: Can you give us one player on each side of the ball who Raider Nation might not know much about but could make an impact in this game? A couple of the Broncos' up-and-comers, if you will.

A: On offense, let's go with rookie running back Jonah Coleman. Though I'm pretty sure Las Vegas fans know of Coleman. But he is what makes Denver's running game click. Coleman's has good vision, good speed, and runs with power. He's been fun to watch, and I would expect him to get more carries on Sunday.

On defense, there really isn't anybody who Raiders Nation hasn't heard of. So I'll go with cornerback Ja'Quan McMillian. The fourth-year undrafted defensive back has a knack for making a key play when Denver needs it, whether an interception or a sack.

#2838. Lewis Hamilton questions stewards in unaired radio message after F1 Vegas penalty

Lewis Hamilton's recovery drive at the 2026 Las Vegas Grand Prix hit a major snag in the opening half of the race - and prompted a pointed complaint over Ferrari team radio regarding FIA stewarding consistency.

Starting 11th after both Ferraris were eliminated in Q2 on Friday night, Hamilton climbed into the points during a fiercely contested opening stint alongside team-mate Charles Leclerc and McLaren's Lando Norris. Around Lap 13, while fighting for seventh place through the Turn 7–8 chicane, Hamilton ran into the run-off area and cut the corner while duelling Norris.

Although space at the apex appeared tight as the McLaren occupied the racing line, stewards determined Hamilton left the track and gained a lasting advantage, imposing a 10-second time penalty.

In a team radio message not broadcast on the global feed, Hamilton expressed clear frustration with the severity of the sanction.

"Isn't that interesting? One race where someone won't have it, and they'll be mitigating circumstances; they give them five seconds, they give me 10 for avoiding an incident," Hamilton told his race engineer.

Hamilton did not name a specific driver or grand prix in his message, yet his reference to "mitigating circumstances" and a reduced five-second penalty immediately recalled a recent flashpoint inside his own team.

Three weeks earlier at the Mexico City Grand Prix on 1 November, Leclerc completed an off-track pass on Hamilton at Turn 4 on the opening lap. Under Formula 1's driving standards guidelines, leaving the track and gaining an advantage carries a standard 10-second penalty. In Mexico, however, the stewards halved Leclerc's sanction to five seconds, explicitly citing mitigation.

"The baseline penalty guideline is 10 seconds; however, in mitigation is the fact that car 44 was also off track on the inside of car 16 and this occurred early in the first lap," the FIA stewards wrote in their Mexico City decision.

Lewis Hamilton, Ferrari, Charles Leclerc, Ferrari

That ruling sparked debate across the paddock at the time. Hamilton - who had served a 10-second penalty for a similar off-track offence in Mexico in 2025 - argued over team radio during the race that the lesser penalty failed to offset the clean-air benefit of staying ahead, while eventual race winner Max Verstappen also remarked afterwards that "normally, that penalty is 10 seconds, and now suddenly it's five".

From a regulatory standpoint, the two rulings hinge on context: stewards routinely apply greater leniency to crowded opening-lap incidents than to mid-race battles, where the full 10-second guideline remains the default.

Still, Hamilton's Las Vegas radio comments prove there is lingering driver frustration over how flexibly those guidelines are applied from weekend to weekend - a sore point the FIA hopes to ease when it begins phasing in full-time Formula 1 stewards in 2027.

#2836. Trump says there could be a 'positive spin' from the latest FBI database hack

President Donald Trump suggested Saturday that there could be a bright spot in the latest incursion into the FBI computer database.

The president confirmed in an interview with Fox News that a system has been breached that houses information used by law enforcement at all levels to share criminal justice information.

The hackers accessed the National Criminal Information Center (NCIC), a database that includes FBI fingerprint records, wanted persons, violent felons, missing persons and related information, two people familiar with the matter told MS NOW. The database is restricted to law enforcement use but not classified and it is unclear what data was extracted.

CNN had first reported on the suspected hack, citing sources familiar with the issue.

In a wide-ranging interview on Fox, Trump said there could be upside from the news.

"Maybe we'll learn about the voter fraud, all the voter fraud that took place," he said. "I always like to put things on a positive spin."

This would be the second recent intrusion into the FBI system; the previous one came in September, when a group known as "ShinyHunters" broke into an FBI jobs website. The result compromised data on current and former FBI employees and shut down part of the agency's operations until the issue was resolved.

Trump said it's possible there could be information gleaned from the latest hack.

"I wonder who's doing it, because wouldn't it be great to learn about Russia, Russia, Russia?" he said. "People should hear about it, because it actually put our country in danger, and so maybe we'll learn things that we wouldn't have learned otherwise."

The FBI did not immediately respond to a CNBC request for comment.

#2832. Anthropic’s record IPO collides with tech’s worst week on Wall Street since 2020

Startup investor Ben Narasin is so jazzed about Anthropic's blockbuster IPO that he's planning a party later this month at his Silicon Valley home. Sake, vodka and Krug Champagne will be served. Each attendee will be given two jars of caviar.

"This is a celebration of the most material, significant, and mind-blowingly awesome event that has ever happened in venture capital," Narasin, founding partner of Tenacity Venture Capital, told CNBC in an interview, adding that he didn't even invest in the company.

Anthropic raised a record $93.5 billion in its IPO and an additional $26 billion from tech giants that bought at the offer price. The stock gained 17% in its debut on Thursday, lifting Anthropic's market cap to $1.8 trillion and providing a much-needed windfall for Silicon Valley's startup ecosystem.

It was quite the split-screen moment on Wall Street, which spent the week in an AI-driven tailspin. Led by Nvidia's 23% plunge, which marked the stock's sharpest weekly pullback in 18 years, the Nasdaq slid 10% over the past five trading days in its worst week since the early days of Covid in 2020.

Investors and analysts spent the week debating whether Anthropic will trade alongside AI hardware, as it's reliant on advanced chips, memory and data centers, or whether it will stand alone as the first public pure-play AI developer and a company that's achieved unrivaled growth in less than six years of existence.

That question will likely persist for some time, but Friday's market action showed that Wall Street is skeptical of Anthropic's ability to grow into its lofty valuation. At week's end, the Claude creator's $1.6 trillion fully diluted market cap put it behind just six U.S. companies: Apple, Microsoft, Alphabet, Nvidia, Amazon and SpaceX.

With the tech market in turmoil due to rising interest rates, raging anti-AI sentiment and Nvidia's C-suite crisis, Anthropic's IPO was part exuberance, part relief for an industry hungry for exits.

After venture-backed IPOs yielded $73 billion in proceeds in 2021, the amount raised in the next four years totaled just $39 billion, according to data from University of Florida emeritus professor Jay Ritter. In 2021, 126 technology companies went public, compared with 34 in 2025.

Meanwhile, the private market has continued to balloon, with companies raising ever-larger sums at sky-high valuations without the pressures of quarterly earnings. Many startups that had hoped to go public early in the decade fell out of favor in the AI boom. They experienced hefty job cuts, down rounds and acquisitions for pennies on the dollar.

"There was a time in Silicon Valley not that long ago where the prevailing wisdom was the default path should be avoiding going public for as long as possible," said Somesh Dash, a general partner at venture firm IVP. The view was, "it's too long, it's too hard, it's not worth it," he said.

Anthropic is very young compared to some of those companies, but it was a huge collector of private capital. Investors poured $141 billion into the AI lab as of September, according to Anthropic's prospectus.

For a few Silicon Valley firms that contributed to those funding rounds, the returns are unlike anything they've ever seen. At the IPO price, Menlo Ventures and Lightspeed Venture Partners each owned about $25 billion worth of Anthropic stock. Bessemer Venture Partners owned a stake worth roughly $10 billion, partner Byron Deeter said.

Matt Murphy, a partner at Menlo, told CNBC's David Faber on Thursday that his firm has more than five limited partners "that we delivered over $1 billion of returns to."

"At the end of the day, that's really our job, is to get dollars back into foundations and endowments," Murphy said. "There's a lot of pride in being able to do that."

Bessemer's Deeter was also jubilant on Thursday, posting on X that, in addition to being "the largest bet in Bessemer's history," the IPO "is a punctuation mark in the history of both humanity and computation."

That's quite a change from a few years earlier, when Anthropic was hardly generating revenue, and success was far from guaranteed. In 2023, Anthropic's Claude 2 was struggling to keep pace with models from Google and OpenAI. In April 2024, investors at Bessemer wrote in a memo that if Anthropic missed its own targets, there was "a good chance that we lose all or part of our money."

Guru Chahal at Lightspeed told CNBC that Anthropic was clearly the team taking a more measured approach to AI. The company looked beyond Nvidia's dominant AI chips, choosing to use alternatives from Amazon and Google as well. Anthropic said it generated $1.72 in revenue per dollar spent on computing power in the third quarter, up from 68 cents a year earlier.

"It is true that it required lots of capital, but when you dig in and you realize that capital is going toward compute, and this is the team that's positioned to use that compute in the most effective way, then we could connect those dots," Chahal said. He added that those metrics showed it was "going to be a very high-quality business."

Anthropic isn't the only big win for VCs this year.

Five months before Anthropic's Nasdaq debut, SpaceX hit the market in what, until this week, was by far the largest IPO in history. SpaceX, which Elon Musk founded in 2002, produced outsized returns for firms including Sequoia and Andreessen Horowitz.

SpaceX and Anthropic alone will make 2026 a monster year for the venture industry, easily exceeding any other year in IPO proceeds. And investors in OpenAI hope appetite remains strong for trillion-dollar companies next year, as the ChatGPT maker is looking to 2027 for a potential offering.

IVP's Dash said that in his AI portfolio, "a lot of companies are starting IPO prep" even if it's only "year four, year five, year six since inception." He hopes to see at least half a dozen companies go public at valuations north of $10 billion each in the second half of 2027.

Jensen Huang's sudden departure from Nvidia has left the chip giant without a clear leader at a time when the company already faced heightened concerns about a slowdown in spending. Nvidia is a key investor in Anthropic and OpenAI and a critical supplier and financing partner for the entire AI industry, which is getting hammered across the board.

In January, Democrats will take control of the House and Senate after this month's midterm elections, which included the passage of numerous ballot measures that could stymie data center development. Anthropic investor Brad Gerstner predicted after the election that market dynamics would push Anthropic to price toward the lower end of its range.

Narasin said he wasn't even sure the company would get that far.

"I was concerned they'd delay," Narasin said.

That makes the outcome that much more impressive, he said, after Anthropic priced above its expected range and upsized the offering.

"This will be bedtime stories for years for future VCs," Narasin said. "There was a company that was created, backed by angels and later venture" that in a few short years "became the single biggest IPO of all history."

— CNBC's Ari Levy contributed to this report.

#2830. 49ers’ Kaelon Black manifested NFL future with Kyle Shanahan

Kaelon Black is making a name for himself by putting together a strong rookie year for the 49ers, proving to be a perfect supporting piece to Christian McCaffrey.

Black was the lead back for the national champion Indiana Hoosiers last year, but he always had his mind on one thing.

49ers running back Kaelon Black celebrating with his teammates in an NFL game. AP Photo/Jeffrey McWhorter

Playing in the NFL and playing for a Shanahan.

On Thursday, he appeared on the NFL Network show "Good Morning Football" and told host Jamie Erdahl that he wrote in a book as a kid that he would play in the NFL under a Shanahan.

"It's funny because I did," Black said. "I was probably in like fourth grade in Washington at the time, and at the time, Mike Shanahan was the coach and I was saying, like, 'I'm going to be the first overall pick drafted by Mike Shanahan. I'm going to play with [Robert Griffin III]' and all this other stuff. I was one of those kids who just dreamed big, and I knew one day that I was going to be in the NFL."

While Black had to watch former Hoosiers teammate Fernando Mendoza be selected with the No. 1 overall pick, the 25-year-old running back was taken by San Francisco in the third round and has found himself a role in the NFL.

This year, Black has 79 carries for 356 yards and two touchdowns — both happened over the 49ers' last three games.

In last week's 46-40 win against the Cowboys, Black had one of his most impressive runs of his young NFL career, when he ran a 25-yard touchdown down the field and started it by running through Caleb Downs, the No. 11 overall pick in the 2026 draft, when he lowered his shoulder on the Ohio State product.

Kaelon Black running downfield in an NFL game against the Cowboys. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Kaelon Black running through Cowboys cornerback Cobie Durant. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Black then got himself back into position and immediately shoved Cowboys cornerback Cobie Durant to the ground to finish the play.

Black's championship-caliber DNA has transitioned to the NFL. The 49ers enter Week 11 with an 8-1 record — the NFC's best — and appear to be true Super Bowl contenders.

#2829. Clippers Fade Late Again in Dallas as Their Fourth-Quarter Problem Grows

The Clippers got the game right where they wanted it with a little more than five minutes to go Saturday night in Dallas, then watched it slip away again in a 110-100 loss to the Mavericks at American Airlines Center.

Kris Dunn hit a corner 3-pointer that cut the Dallas lead to 91-88 with 5:12 remaining, but the Mavericks answered with an 11-0 run helped along by two Clippers turnovers, and the visitors never threatened again. Kyrie Irving opened the night 1 of 7 from the field and still finished with 25 points, scoring 12 of them in the fourth quarter to close it out.

The loss dropped the Clippers to 4-11 overall and 0-7 on the road, and they have now lost three in a row since a home loss to Utah on Tuesday.

Brook Lopez sparked an early 9-0 burst, and the Clippers led 26-23 after one quarter before the second quarter went sideways on them. Dallas ripped off a 13-0 run in that period, won the quarter 29-18 and took a 52-44 lead into halftime.

The Clippers pushed back with a 30-point third quarter and trimmed the gap to 78-74 heading into the fourth, which set up the same closing stretch that has haunted this group all month. Dallas won the final period 32-26, and the Clippers finished with 16 turnovers that the Mavericks turned into 22 points.

Cooper Flagg had 22 points and eight assists for Dallas even though he went 7 of 13 at the free-throw line, the first time last season's Rookie of the Year has missed six free throws in a game. Walter Clayton Jr. added 16 points off the bench, Max Christie scored 13 and Daniel Gafford posted 10 points and 10 rebounds while the Dallas reserves outscored the Clippers' bench 42-35. The Mavericks played without starting forward P.J. Washington Jr., who sat out with an illness.

Nov 21, 2026; Dallas, Texas, USA; LA Clippers center Brook Lopez (11) looks to move the ball past Dallas Mavericks forward Daniel Gafford (21) during the second half at the American Airlines Center.

Nov 21, 2026; Dallas, Texas, USA; LA Clippers center Brook Lopez (11) looks to move the ball past Dallas Mavericks forward Daniel Gafford (21) during the second half at the American Airlines Center.

Lopez led the Clippers with 21 points, scoring 17 of them after halftime to keep his team within reach through the third quarter. Rui Hachimura added 19 points and made four 3-pointers to match his season high.

Darius Garland had 12 of his 16 points by halftime but went quiet after the break. The rest of the rotation had a hard time finding the basket, with Derrick Jones Jr. going 1 of 7 and Keaton Wagler going 1 of 8 as the Clippers shot 38.4% from the field and 15 of 45 from deep.

The Clippers were again without Bradley Beal, who is managing his right knee, along with Brandon Ingram and Jordan Miller.

I have watched this team hang around long enough to make things interesting and then come apart once the game asks for some composure, and it has happened far too many times this month. The Clippers led Utah 62-58 at halftime on Tuesday and lost by 25 after the Jazz poured in 44 points in the fourth quarter. They stayed with San Antonio deep into Thursday night before the Spurs won the final period 32-24, and Saturday in Dallas followed the same script.

Across the three games of this losing streak, opponents have outscored the Clippers 108-71 in fourth quarters, which is a gap that points at focus and execution far more than talent.

What bothers me most is who is on the floor when it happens. Lopez, Garland, Dunn, Hachimura and Jones have all played in big games in this league, and veterans with that much experience should be the ones who settle things down late and get a stop when the other side makes its push. Instead, the late turnovers keep piling up, the defense keeps breaking down in the final minutes, and the hole in the standings gets deeper with every loss.

Ingram is set to return Monday, and his ability to create his own shot late in games is exactly what this offense has been missing during these collapses. One player cannot fix a habit the whole group has fallen into, though, and the veterans in that locker room have to start owning the closing minutes before this season gets away from them.

The Clippers continue their road trip Monday at Houston, with tipoff set for 5:30 p.m. PT at Toyota Center.

#2828. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2827. Tom Brady, after selling Birmingham City stake, buys piece of Chelsea

Tom Brady’s exit from the English soccer business was only temporary.

After selling his stake in Birmingham City, Brady has reportedly purchased a slice of the Chelsea soccer club, albeit indirectly.

Via ESPN, Brady will become a minority investor in Clearlake Capital, the entity that controls Chelsea.

Separate reporting from the Times characterizes the situation as Brady being “in talks” to take a “small minority stake” within the portion of Clearlake Capital that owns Chelsea. Brady, per the Times, also would become a member of the board of advisors.

Whether that means he’ll eventually be criticizing the work ethic of the team’s manager remains to be seen.

The end result is that Brady hasn’t streamlined his overall portfolio in an effort to achieve more “balance” in his life. Instead, he’s swapping out his piece of a second-tier club for interest in a member of the Premier League.

#2826. NFL Week 11 games: Eagles rooting guide

The eleventh weekend of the 2026 NFL season is here!

Let's run through a Philadelphia Eagles-focused rooting guide for all of the Week 11 games.

The 2-7 Eagles have a 0.7% chance of making the playoffs, according to FTN Fantasy. They're +4000 to qualify for the postseason on FanDuel, which means you can bet $10 to win $400 if that happens.

In other words, it's time to be interested in the Eagles maximizing their positioning in the 2027 NFL Draft.

Current draft order of non-playoff teams via Tankathon:

I can understand fans not wanting the Birds to lose while the game is ongoing, or at all.

But the reality is that the 2026 campaign is lost and the Eagles stand to benefit beyond this season by losing and getting the highest draft pick as possible.

With a loss to the Steelers, the Eagles can potentially move as high as No. 2 overall.

INDIANAPOLIS COLTS defeated by HOUSTON TEXANS: The Colts lost on Thursday night, which wasn't ideal. They're a threat to move ahead of Philly in the draft order, especially since their weak strength of schedule will likely give them a tiebreaker over the Eagles if they finish with the same record. The silver lining to a Texans win is that it keeps Houston mathematically alive to win the AFC South. The Eagles would prefer to see the Texans win their division instead of the Jags, since Philly owns Jacksonville first-round pick.

JACKSONVILLE JAGUARS at NEW YORK GIANTS: Thanks to the Jordan Davis trade, the Eagles own the Jaguars' first-round pick in the 2027 NFL Draft. Therefore, the Eagles want the Jags to lose as much as possible. The "bonus" to a Giants win is that it helps to prevent any false hope of the Eagles winning their division. Root for the Giants.

TENNESSEE TITANS at DALLAS COWBOYS: Two reasons to root for the Titans here: 1) always great to see the Cowboys lose and 2) the Eagles need the Titans to win to have a chance to move ahead of them in the draft order.

BALTIMORE RAVENS at CAROLINA PANTHERS: Thanks to the Cam Jurgens trade, the Eagles own Baltimore's fifth-round pick in the 2027 NFL Draft. Therefore, the Eagles want the Ravens to lose as much as possible. Root for the Panthers.

MIAMI DOLPHINS at BUFFALO BILLS: The Eagles need the Dolphins to win to have a chance to move ahead of them in the draft order. Root for the Dolphins.

NEW ORLEANS SAINTS at CHICAGO BEARS: The Saints are the bigger threat to move ahead of the Eagles in the draft order. Root for the Saints.

TAMPA BAY BUCCANEERS at DETROIT LIONS: The Bucs are the bigger threat to move ahead of the Eagles in the draft order. Root for the Buccaneers.

ARIZONA CARDINALS at KANSAS CITY CHIEFS: The Cards are the bigger threat to move ahead of the Eagles in the draft order. Root for the Cardinals.

NEW YORK JETS at LOS ANGELES CHARGERS: The good news for the Eagles is that one of these two teams currently ahead of them in the draft order will win (barring a tie). Looking at the remaining schedules for these teams (which both rank near the middle of the pack in terms of difficulty), I think the Chargers are actually more likely to win more games after this week. So, it could be good to see the Jets capitalize on a more rare opportunity for them to win. Root for the Jets.

LAS VEGAS RAIDERS at DENVER BRONCOS: The Raiders are the bigger threat to move ahead of the Eagles in the draft order. Root for the Raiders.

MINNESOTA VIKINGS at SAN FRANCISCO 49ERS: The Whiners are the less likable team, in my view. But there's a case to be made the Eagles should want the Packers to win the NFC North (and, ultimately, the Super Bowl) since the Cowboys own Green Bay's first-round pick. Root for the 49ers.

CINCINNATI BENGALS at WASHINGTON COMMANDERS: The Commanders are the bigger threat to move ahead of the Eagles in the draft order. Root for the Commanders.
