LeBron James (21 points), Tyrese Maxey (17 points) and Anfernee Simons (18) were among the seven Sixers to score in double figures.

The Sixers were down Jaylen Brown (left hamstring strain), Dean Wade (left hamstring tightness/soreness), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear).

Next up for the Sixers is a Monday night meeting with the Trail Blazers. Here are observations on their victory over the Bucks:

Maxey returned to the Sixers' starting five after missing the team's comeback win Wednesday over the Magic with right foot soreness. He was joined by VJ Edgecombe, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, James and Embiid.

James threw down a dunk on the day's first play. Embiid then dished to a cutting Edgecombe for a layup that put the Sixers up 4-0.

The Bucks quickly found a foothold in the game.

Embiid missed his first four shots, including two that were swatted by Myles Turner. Maxey began 1 for 5 from the floor. Herro canned a three-pointer to give Milwaukee a 15-8 lead.

The Sixers entered the game with a minus-13.2 net rating in first quarters, which ranked 28th in the NBA. They faced a 30-29 deficit after Saturday's first period.

Even with Maxey available again, Labaron Philon Jr. deservingly stayed in the Sixers' rotation following an excellent outing in Orlando.

He scored two points in six first-half minutes. The 20-year-old made a rookie mistake at the tail end of the first quarter, fouling Herro on a three-point attempt.

Dominick Barlow checked in for his first action of the season with 2:51 remaining in the first quarter. He'd been sidelined for the Sixers' first 16 games because of a lower back stress reaction.

Barlow scored his first points early in the second quarter when he tipped in a Maxey miss. He then teamed up with James in crowd-pleasing style, sprinting ahead of the pack on a fast break and flushing home the 22-time All-Star's long-distance lob.

The afternoon was not devoid of rust for Barlow. Shortly after that alley-oop, he grabbed an offensive rebound, dribbled out to the perimeter and fired an open three-pointer … which he air balled short.

To Barlow's credit, he took another three early in the fourth quarter and drained it. He logged 11 minutes and was quite productive, tallying 12 points on 5-for-6 shooting and four rebounds.

"It felt great," Barlow said of his three. "Got to shoot the ball with confidence. … I've obviously got to get a game feel; I've been missing that. But I worked too hard (in the offseason) to not shoot the ball when I'm open."

Kyle Kuzma hit a three late in the second quarter to build the Bucks' lead to 59-52. After Sixers head coach Nick Nurse's timeout, the team closed the first half on a 8-0 run featuring an Embiid dunk.

The Sixers went just 3 for 16 from long range in the first half, but their low-turnover ways helped mitigate those shooting struggles.

They committed a single turnover in the first half and finished with four giveaways. That's tied for their lowest ever on record in a single game. The Sixers have the NBA's lowest turnover percentage outside of garbage time, according to Cleaning the Glass.

"No matter who you're playing against, teams try to turn you over, they try to speed you up," James said. "Us taking care of the ball, that's a lot of the reason why we won this game."

The Sixers' shooters were bound to start sinking some open looks eventually. Simons, Caldwell-Pope and Edgecombe all made threes late in the third quarter and the team's lead grew as high as 13 points.

Thanks largely to Herro's individuals exploits, the Bucks stayed competitive. AJ Green buried a corner three to cut Milwaukee's deficit to 106-103 with 8:10 to go in the game.

James sought out his shot much more following the Sixers' timeout and did so with great success. He scored four buckets inside over the next four minutes, including a big slam that put the Sixers up 121-112.

It appeared the main questions left were whether Embiid could reach a triple-double and just how many points Herro would rack up.

However, the Bucks forced the Sixers to work down the stretch. Herro got to the 50-point milestone and trimmed the Sixers' lead to two points when he converted a layup with 19.2 seconds left.

The Sixers safely navigated to the final buzzer by the slimmest of margins. Embiid made four late free throws and everyone on the Sixers' sideline was relieved to see that Herro hadn't quite released his jumper in time.

"I try not to complain too much about any win in this league," Nurse said. "The message is we've got to finish a little better."

#2784. Dwight Howard makes shocking NBA trade admission that would have sent Stephen Curry to Magic

Dwight Howard makes shocking NBA trade admission that would have sent Stephen Curry to Magic appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

When the Golden State Warriors drafted Stephen Curry in the 2009 NBA Draft, there was skepticism around an immediate impact on the league from the point guard. And Dwight Howard was no different.

The former Orlando Magic star turned down a trade for Curry back in 2011 when the front office proposed one. But more than a decade after the missed opportunity, Howard is regretting his past decision.

The former NBA center recently revisited the decision during an appearance on "The AM Hoops Show," where he recalled Orlando's interest in Curry and his own preference for Monta Ellis at the time.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

"The Magic wanted to get Steph, and I said no," Howard recalled. "I said no because I didn't know. I didn't know Wardell Steph Curry was gonna be one of the greatest shooters in the world."

Howard's preference for Ellis was understandable at the time. Ellis was an established scorer in Golden State, while Curry was dealing with recurring ankle injuries that limited his availability. Curry appeared in just 26 games during the 2011-12 season, adding uncertainty to his long-term outlook.

The Warriors eventually traded Ellis to Milwaukee in March 2012, keeping Curry as part of their plans. Curry underwent another ankle procedure that April before signing a four-year extension with Golden State that offseason.

The decision paid off. Curry averaged 22.9 points per game in 2012-13 and helped the Warriors reach the playoffs. He eventually became a two-time MVP and led Golden State to four NBA championships.

Howard's time in Orlando was also nearing its end. After requesting a trade, the eight-time All-Star eventually left the Magic for the Los Angeles Lakers in August 2012, ending an eight-season run with the franchise.

#2780. The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats!

The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats! originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

This 76ers team is a heck of a lot of fun to watch and fun basketball means fun stats!

At 13-3, the 76ers are exactly a month into the 5th-best start in franchise history and best since they were 14-2 in 2000-01. Which turned out to be a pretty good year. They were 15-1 in 1949-50 and 1966-67 and 14-2 in 1981-82.

To help everybody get through this interminable two-day gap between 76ers games, here's our first 2026-27 installment of Roob's 76ers Stats, with a tip of the hat to Stathead.

→ The 76ers have been out-rebounded by 34 through 16 games (719-685) and their 13-3 record matches the best in NBA history by a team that's been out-rebounded after 16 games. Three other teams have been 13-3 despite getting out-rebounded, most recently the 2013-14 Miami Heat. The others are the 1979-80 Bucks and 2012-2013 Spurs. The 76ers are an NBA-best 6-3 this year when they've been out-rebounded (which means they're 7-0 when they have a rebounding edge).

→ In the win over Orlando Thursday, V.J. Edgecombe had 24 points, eight rebounds, six assists and two steals, and Joel Embiid had 26 points, six rebounds, seven assists and two steals. It was the first time two 76ers had at least 24 points, six rebounds and assists and two steals in a game in 53 years. In a win over the Celtics at the Spectrum in 1973, Fred Carter had 31 points, six rebounds, six assists and seven steals and George McGinniss had 28 points, 16 rebounds, eight assists and three steals.

→ Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, a career 37 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 44 percent so far. Anfernee Simons, a career 38 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 41 percent. Jabari Walker, a career 33 percent 3-point shooter, is at 42 percent. Dean Wade, a career 37 percent shooter, is at 41 percent. Jaylen Brown, a career 36 percent shooter from range, is at 39 percent. The 76ers are shooting just 36 percent as a team from the stripe because James and Embiid have been so bad, but everybody else is off to career-best starts from 3-point range.

→ The 76ers are 7-1 on the road, equalling their best road record ever after eight games. They were also 7-1 in 1980-81, 1981-82 and 1982-83. The 1981 team had the best road start in franchise history, going 12-1 before losing its second road game.

→ With 15.7 points, 6.8 assists and 6.8 rebounds per game, LeBron James is only the fifth 76er ever to average at least 15 ½ points and 6 ½ assists and rebounds through 16 games. The others are some familiar names: Wilt Chamberlain in 1967, Charles Barkley in 1987, Ben Simmons in 2018 and James Harden in 2023.

→ The 76ers already have more wins this year after trailing by 10 points through the first quarter than all but two full seasons since 1950. They had four in 1985-86 and again in 2022-23. The Hawks also have three wins this year when trailing by double digits after the first quarter, but they're 3-4 and the 76ers are 3-2. So they actually have a winning record when they're down by double digits going into the second quarter.

→ With Jaylen Brown (22.6, 6.6), Joel Embiid (20.0, 8.3), LeBron James (15.7, 6.8) and V.J. Edgecombe (13.6, 6.5), the 76ers are the only NBA team with four players averaging 6 ½ rebounds and 13 points. The last time they had four players in a season who averaged 13 points and 6 ½ rebounds was 1961-62 when they were the Syracuse Nationals, with Dave Gambee (16.7, 7.9), Hal Greer (22.8, 7.4), Red Kerr (16.3, 14.7), Dolph Schayes (14.7, 7.8) and Lee Shaffer (16.9, 6.8).

→ Edgecombe's 6.5 rebounds per game are 2nd-most among shooting guards behind only the Grizzlies' Cedric Coward, who is at 8.5. Edgecombe's 6.5 is also 2nd-highest ever by a 76ers shooting guard behind only Greer's 7.4 during that 1961-62 season. Edgecombe averaged 5.6 rebounds as a rookie and his 5.8 career average is highest by a shooting guard who spent more than one year with the 76ers. Greer is second at 5.3. Among all NBA players who've played at least 90 games, Edgecombe's 5.8 average is 11th-highest all-time among shooting guards (although Stathead includes some swing guard-forwards in their data).

→ Joel Embiid's 14.6 shooting percentage from 3 is worst in NBA history after 16 games (minimum 40 attempts). Embiid is 6-for-41 from the stripe this year. The previous 3-point shooting low 16 games into a season belonged to Cedric Ceballos of the Lakers in 1995-96 and the 76ers' Evan Turner in 2013-14. Both were 6-for-43 for 16.3 percent.

→ LeBron James is at 22.2 percent from 3 on 10-for-45. The Sixers are the first NBA team in 12 years with two players under 23 percent after 16 games with a minimum of 40 attempts. In 2014 Tony Wroten was 10-for-50 (10 percent) and Turner was 6-for-43 (16.3 percent).

#2779. 'Came, saw and delivered': Cricket world hails Rishabh Pant as he scores century on comeback for India in Tests

'Came, saw and delivered': Cricket world hails Rishabh Pant as he scores century on comeback for India in Tests originally appeared on Cricket News. Add Cricket News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Rishabh Pant made 128 off 153 balls on his return to Test cricket at Wellington.

It was his first Test hundred in New Zealand and his ninth overall.

The Indian wicketkeeper is the first Asian wicketkeeper with Test centuries in all four SENA countries.

Rishabh Pant had not finished a competitive innings since August when he walked out at the Basin Reserve, and the lead-up had not been kind. His last completed Test was the second against Sri Lanka in Colombo; a side strain ended his Irani Cup in October before the match was over, and he arrived in New Zealand without a single India A game behind him.

What followed was 128 from 153 balls with 10 fours and six sixes, an innings that took India past 600 and set up the declaration on 720 for 9 with a lead of 352.

He reached three figures in the manner that has defined him, skipping down at Glenn Phillips on 99 and hitting him into the stands over long-off rather than taking the single on offer.

The hundred was his ninth in Tests, and seven of those have come in SENA conditions, which makes him the first wicketkeeper from Asia with a Test century in England, Australia, South Africa, and New Zealand. The six that brought it up was also his 108th in Tests, moving him past Brendon McCullum into second on the all-time list behind Ben Stokes.

Rachin Ravindra eventually had him caught by Nathan Smith at deep midwicket off the last ball before lunch on day four, and Pant walked off to a standing ovation from a full Sunday crowd in Wellington.

MORE: IND vs NZ 1st Test 2026 live scorecard, toss winner, winning probability, result and highlights

Among the first to respond was the broadcaster Vikrant Gupta, who wrote, "Rishabh Pant came. Saw. And delivered yet again! India resume with a lead of 261 in double quick time, and I would expect a declaration in the next hour or so at max."

Former India batter Mohammad Kaif made the case for where the innings sits in Pant's career, posting, "Rishabh Pant is a genuine Test-match giant, and his greatness goes beyond statistics. Pant's innings have changed the direction of important Test matches, What a player."

The shot that brought up the hundred drew the loudest response, with one viewer picking up on Ian Smith's observation in commentary and writing, "Agree with Ian Smith on air who says Rishabh Pant can take a single anywhere in the ground, but what does he do at 99* steps out and hits a six? This is what makes him Box Office."

The SENA record was what caught another supporter, who posted, "Rishabh Pant has a SENA test century in each of the four countries now. Blimey, this lad is going to end up with the GOAT status in this format, most probably."

Others went back to how little preparation he had, with this fan writing, "Landed just 90 hours before the Test series. Missed India A because his simulation was pushed back by a week amid the CoE mess. They ran negative PR against him, yet he owned it when it mattered."

GS Vivek, consulting editor at ABP News, praised the innings while putting the total in context, writing, "Rishabh Pant scores WHEN and WHERE he needs to score. Time and again. Don't know when was the last time India scored 700+ in a Test match and to do that in NZ means two things, that this is the flattest pitch in NZ and also the most toothless."

MORE: Pakistan's recent win in Asia Cup 2026 for blind embroils in new controversy

The innings answers a selection argument that had been building all year. Dhruv Jurel kept wicket in Pant's absence and has not stopped scoring, and he made 78 from 79 balls in this same innings while batting as a specialist, so India are now carrying two keepers who both have to play.

Pant's case for the gloves rests on exactly what Wellington produced. Six of his nine Test hundreds have come away from home, and the only one on this tour came from a man who had faced no competitive bowling for three months.

The qualification in GS Vivek's post is the fair one, because the Basin Reserve surface gave the bowlers nothing across four days, and six India batters passed fifty on it. What it does not explain is why the man with the least preparation on the side scored the fastest hundred on it.

#2776. Observations after Sixers come back for strong shorthanded victory

Observations after Sixers come back for strong shorthanded victory originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stretched their winning streak to four games and snagged a strong road win Wednesday night over the Magic.

They earned a 121-116 victory in Orlando to improve to 13-3 on the season. The Magic dropped to 6-8.

Joel Embiid had 26 points, seven assists, six rebounds, three blocks and two steals.

VJ Edgecombe posted a season-high 24 points, eight boards and six assists.

LeBron James scored 22 points and Anfernee Simons added 18.

Magic star Franz Wagner had 27 points, 11 assists and eight rebounds.

The Sixers had a long injury list in Orlando. Tyrese Maxey (right foot soreness), Jaylen Brown (left hamstring strain) and Dean Wade (left hamstring soreness/tightness) were among their players out. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse told reporters in Orlando that Brown will be re-evaluated when the team returns to Philadelphia.

Orlando was without star forward Paolo Banchero (right high ankle sprain).

The Sixers will start a three-game homestand when they face the Bucks on Saturday afternoon. Here are observations on their win over the Magic:

The Sixers' undermanned starting lineup was Edgecombe, Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, James and Embiid.

After scoring 37 points in the Sixers' win over the Nets, Embiid had a subpar start. He began 1 for 6 from the floor and committed a turnover that led to an Anthony Black dunk.

The Sixers' lineup looked exactly like a unit that hadn't built any chemistry yet. The team was not sharp early on either end of the floor.

Desmond Bane drained a long three-pointer to give Orlando a 27-12 advantage. Orlando made 11 of its first 14 field goals and led by as many as 17 points in the first quarter.

Labaron Philon Jr. and Justin Edwards joined the Sixers' rotation. So did two-way contract players Tacko Fall and Caleb Love.

Philon impressed in the first quarter, converting two floaters and a corner three. The rookie passed his previous NBA scoring high of five points and finished the night with 10 on 4-for-5 shooting.

Fall played 10 minutes and posted two points, three rebounds and three blocks. He slammed in a dunk and rejected two shots early in the second quarter. Credit to Fall for giving the Sixers a good backup center performance and doing his job as a rim-protecting ultra-big man.

Love entered in the second quarter and was ready to fire, hitting a catch-and-shoot three-pointer on his first touch.

The Sixers' zone defense also helped them eat into the Magic's lead. The zone seemed to disrupt Orlando's offensive rhythm and it was active enough to prevent the Magic from consistently creating high-quality looks.

Edgecombe's buzzer-beating mid-range jumper cut the Sixers' deficit to 69-64 after two quarters.

The Sixers pulled within a single point on multiple occasions in the third quarter, including on a Philon three late in the period. They trailed by five points after the third.

Fall continued to play well early in the fourth quarter. He deterred the Magic's drivers from getting all the way to the rim as the very large back line of the Sixers' zone defense and made eye-catching hustle plays on both sides of the ball.

The Sixers outscored Orlando by seven points on the night with Fall on the court. Soon after Embiid subbed back in, he evened the game at 104-all with two free throws. Jabari Walker swished a three to finally push the Sixers in front.

The Magic regained a late lead with a Jalen Suggs three. James then drove and kicked the ball out to Edgecombe, who nailed one of his customary clutch jumpers to put the Sixers back on top.

No matter the night-to-night personnel, the Sixers have been fantastic at the tail end of close games. They're now 11-0 in clutch situations, which the NBA defines as games within a five-point differential in the final five minutes.

#2775. Stanford comes up big against Cal, becomes bowl eligible with win in Berkeley

The 129th iteration of the Big Game was perhaps one of the strangest.

In Stanford's 44-28 win over Cal on Saturday night at Memorial Stadium, the Cardinal's second consecutive win in the storied rivalry - which made them bowl eligible for the first time since 2018 - Bears head coach Tosh Lupoi unveiled a befuddling new offense. Initially thought to be a "wrinkle" to Cal's usual offense, as described on the ACC Network telecast in the first quarter, it quickly became clear that Lupoi was instead soft-launching another benching of sophomore quarterback Jaron-Keawe Sagapolutele.

Cal sprinkled in Sagapolutele, the quarterback whom fans had placed their hopes and dreams on before the season, on some drives in the first half. But not often. The Bears' starting quarterback largely stood on the sideline, with his helmet on, while third-string quarterback Dominic Ingrassia either handed the ball off or took quarterback keepers. When Sagapolutele did take the field, he wasn't involved in the action. He would call a play in the huddle, line up as a wide receiver and watch Cal's running back take a direct snap.

Near the end of the second quarter, the Bears let Sagapolutele pass the ball. He threw a touchdown pass to wide receiver Ian Strong three plays later. The next drive, with Cal having an opportunity to take a lead entering halftime, Strong fumbled Sagapolutele's fourth pass. Stanford returned it 52 yards and kicked a field goal to enter the half up 17-14, and Cal returned to its option-heavy offense to start the second half.

By the time Sagapolutele threw his next pass, Cal (3-8, 2-6 ACC) trailed 30-14, and the game was largely out of reach. Stanford (6-5, 4-4) dominated the third quarter, stringing together a pair of touchdown drives - and another early in the fourth - while Cal punted on three straight possessions.

The immediate reasoning behind Cal's offensive shift was unclear. Stanford head coach Tavita Pritchard called the strategy "unconventional" in his TV interview early in the second half. Lupoi, asked about the new look in the first half, told the broadcast: "We're just doing everything we can to win the game."

Cal, which dropped its fifth consecutive game, had begun working Ingrassia into the fold during practices in the lead-up to Saturday, ACC Network broadcasters Justin Kutcher and Max Browne revealed. Cal general manager Ron Rivera, in a KNBR radio interview earlier this week, admitted his team lacked an identity. He met with Lupoi during the week to discuss what they wanted that identity to be going forward, and he mentioned his desire to establish an aggressive, physical brand of football.

"We go into this game with really nothing to lose," Rivera said Thursday.

That could help explain the Bears' run-heavy approach Saturday. They called 24 rushes to just six passes in the first half. Sagapolutele aired it out 17 times in the second half, once the game was broken open.

It wasn't just Cal's offense that struggled. The Bears, who rank near the bottom of the country in penalties, committed a whopping 12 infractions for 107 yards. A first-quarter pass interference call on cornerback Jae'on Young gifted Stanford the ball on the 2-yard line and led to its first touchdown of the night. Cal also struggled on special teams, allowing Stanford to average 35 yards on their two kick returns; one went for 40 yards, setting the Cardinal up for a three-play, 44-yard scoring drive out of the break.

Stanford continued to impress, earning its fourth win in its past five games under first-year head coach Tavita Pritchard. The Cardinal totaled 412 yards of offense, including 196 yards on the ground on 33 attempts (5.9 yards per carry). Running back Micah Ford ran the ball 20 times for 151 yards and two scores.

#2771. Is Drew Mestemaker Playing Tonight? Oklahoma State QB Injury Update

Oklahoma State quarterback Drew Mestemaker is a true game-time decision tonight at Arizona State, with a right shoulder injury threatening to sideline the most productive passer in college football. About two hours before game time, however, the 20-year-old Cowboys QB was seen warming up on the field with no visible shoulder wrapping or anything else indicating the injury.

With the top-rated quarterback in the 2027 NFL Draft class in doubt, questions about how he got hurt, and what it costs him, arrive all at once.

Mestemaker traveled with the team, and Oklahoma State posted photos of a smiling Mestemaker boarding the plane. Coach Eric Morris said Tuesday that he expected his quarterback to be "OK," according to ClutchPoints. Morris added that the Cowboys will play him only if he is comfortable, according to the Pistols Firing Blog.

How to Watch Drew Mestemaker Live: Oklahoma State vs. Arizona State, TV, Stream

The injury happened early in last Saturday's 52-27 loss to Texas Tech. Mestemaker mishandled a snap on third-and-9, and a Red Raiders defensive lineman hit him as he picked up the ball. He landed on his right, throwing shoulder.

"I just landed on my shoulder weird," Mestemaker said. "Just a stinger."

He kept playing, even as trainers worked on the shoulder twice in the first quarter. Mestemaker finished 27-of-42 for 277 yards with one touchdown and two interceptions before true freshman Broderick Vehrs replaced him with 8:12 left. Vehrs starts if Mestemaker can't play.

No diagnosis beyond the stinger has surfaced, and no earlier injury appears in the coverage of his high school, North Texas or Oklahoma State career. That makes the shoulder the first health issue on his record.

So what does it do to his draft position? Arizona Sports described his 10 interceptions as a real concern for scouts and the stinger as worth monitoring.

ESPN's Mel Kiper Jr. ranks Mestemaker No. 2 overall behind Jeremiah Smith, the Ohio State receiver, and first among quarterbacks. Dane Brugler of The Athletic also has him QB1 and No. 2 on a board published Nov. 12, three days before the injury.

ESPN's Pete Thamel polled NFL scouts and executives in October, and they named Mestemaker the consensus first quarterback off the board. A Bleacher Report mock draft has him going third to the Miami Dolphins, and its scouting report places him ninth overall.

Mestemaker leads all FBS quarterbacks with 3,239 passing yards and 30 touchdowns through 10 games, completing 66.4% of his throws with 10 interceptions.

"He can throw with no space; he does not need space to throw," Arizona State coach Kenny Dillingham said.

Mestemaker, 6-foot-3 and 215 pounds, turns 21 in January with 20 career starts, Kiper noted. At Vandegrift High School in Austin, he was a backup quarterback who earned all-district honors as a punter and safety. He drew no scholarship offers, so he walked on at North Texas after a workout.

In 2025, he led the Mean Green to a 12-2 season, throwing for a nation-leading 4,379 yards and 34 touchdowns. He won the 2025 Burlsworth Trophy as the top former walk-on and was named American Conference Offensive Player of the Year. After following Morris to Oklahoma State, he led the Cowboys past No. 6 Oregon 39-31 in September.

Would NFL teams see him as an injury risk? The Bleacher Report scouting report lists turnovers, slower post-snap processing and a habit of carrying the ball low among his weaknesses. Durability does not appear on that list.

The final availability report arrives 90 minutes before the 7:15 p.m. PST, 10:15 p.m. EST, kickoff. It will settle whether the stinger sidelines the nation's passing leader.

#2770. Jets have chance to ‘pick’ apart interception-happy Justin Herbert

LOS ANGELES — What happens when a team that rarely gets interceptions plays a quarterback who can't stop throwing interceptions?

The Jets have two interceptions this season after going the entire 2025 season without one. Chargers quarterback Justin Herbert has thrown 11 interceptions this season. He has thrown at least one interception in all but one game this season; that game came last week against the Ravens, which also happened to be the Chargers' first win.

The Jets secondary grabbed their second interception of the season last week against the Bills when cornerback Azareye'h Thomas picked off Josh Allen. Could their third — or even more than one — come this week?

The Jets have not had multiple interceptions in a game since the last game of the 2024 season.

"Anytime a quarterback is getting pressured and having to make tough throws, there's always an opportunity here," Jets safety Minkah Fitzpatrick said. "We just have to do a good job of making him uncomfortable and those opportunities will come."

While the Jets have the fewest interceptions in the NFL this season, they lead the league with 10 fumbles recovered. Getting more interceptions has been a topic of discussion all season for the Jets defense.

Justin Herbert already has thrown 11 touchdowns this season. Getty Images

"I feel like every week that's definitely a focus of ours is to try to get the ball, get some takeaways," safety Dane Belton said. "Coming into games like this, that's what you've got to do to help the team. Looking back at the statistics of us this year, we're doing well on the forced fumbles but we've got to pick it up on the interceptions. Really every game, we're focused on that and if we can turn it around this week it would be awesome."

The Jets have lost five straight games to the Chargers.

Their last win was in 2011. Their last road win was in the 2010 playoffs when the team still played in San Diego.

The Jets elevated WR Sterling Shepard for Sunday's game.

He provides some depth with Omar Cooper Jr. and Isaiah Williams both out with injuries.

#2767. Marcus Freeman Recaps Notre Dame's 24-3 Victory Over SMU On Senior Day

For the first time in his career, Notre Dame head coach Marcus Freeman went undefeated at home. Tonight, the Irish hosted the SMU Mustangs (7-3) and handled business 24-3. It was ugly at times, but the offense did what they needed to in order to win the game. The defense, although they allowed some big plays, stiffened when SMU got into the scoring zone and didn't allow a touchdown. After their 21-point victory, Freeman broke down what this win meant for the program, the seniors on the team and for the season moving forward. The College Football Playoffs are right around the corner, and given this weekend's chaos, winning was the most important thing tonight.

You can watch the entire press conference here:

Here are the main quotes from Freeman's post-game press conference:

"It's really encouraging," Freeman shared. "It's been an area of focus, especially since the bye week. We recognized that was an area we have to get better at. We tried to be intentional about the work we put in. They played well. They played with clarity. They played -- on their side of the line of scrimmage, I thought our D-line did a really good job, and then they matched up in coverage. They covered their guys. It's about execution. They decided not to kick the field goal. So we were able to get a turnover on downs, a couple of turnovers on downs, which were big. It's good to see those guys execute on that play, especially when you have just a couple of yards to go to score."

"It was a battle," Freeman stated. "We never felt comfortable until we win victory. We felt -- it wasn't a one-score game, but they kept battling. I think we scored the first drive of the second half, and nobody scored after that. It was a battle, man. I told Coach Lashlee before the game they're a good team. I watched the film. I saw what happened the first half of last week, and I know what type of team they are, and they came in and showed they're a good, tough football team. That's a really good program, and they've got a good head coach. They'll have continuing success as they move forward."

"It's a reflection of trust," Freeman explained. "Sometimes the quarterback has multiple options to throw the ball, and he'll probably end up throwing the ball to the guy he trusts that's going to be in the right place and probably make the play. That trust is built over time and through the repeated experience of him doing his job and making the play. Some plays are designed for certain people to get open and throw them the ball, but sometimes there's opportunities to throw the ball to multiple people, and you're going to pick the guy you trust."

#2765. CeeDee Lamb fined $23,882 for block the league used as a highlight

When Cowboys wide receiver CeeDee Lamb made a downfield block on a George Pickens touchdown catch Sunday against the 49ers, the league’s website was so impressed that the highlight video on NFL.com emphasized Lamb’s block over Pickens’ touchdown.

“Can’t-Miss Play: CeeDee Lamb’s HUGE block springs Pickens free for 39-yard TD,” the caption on the highlight video says.

Calling the play on Fox, Tom Brady praised Lamb, immediately recognizing it as a great downfield block.

But the league’s disciplinary process didn’t think it was so great. Lamb has been fined $23,882 for what the NFL deemed “Unnecessary Roughness: Use of the helmet.”

Lamb confirmed to reporters on Thursday that he had been informed he was getting fined, but he insisted he didn’t make contact with his helmet and found it frustrating that receivers take hits routinely but get fined when they make one.

“I can block, and I’m real good at doing things like that, but I get fined,” Lamb said. “I never touched him with my helmet. . . . I guess as a receiver, we can get hit, but can’t deliver the punishment.”

Lamb will surely appeal the fine for what looked, both to the league’s website and the league’s all-time greatest player, like a good block.

#2763. Week 11 Preview: #1 Seed on the Line?

The Minnesota Vikings will travel to Mexico City to face the 8-1 San Francisco 49ers in a game that could very well determine the #1 seed in the NFC playoff bracket in January.

With the Vikings currently tied with the 7-2 Detroit Lions and Chicago Bears in the ridiculously good NFC North, a win over the 49ers could put them in the driver's seat for the rest of the way. After Sunday, the schedule gets much easier, with five of the final seven games at U.S. Bank Stadium. The Vikings also have the benefit of already being 3-1 in the division, with the final two at home. The Lions and Bears each have four division games left. And while the Packers may "just" be 6-4 and destined for last place, they're certainly no pushovers.

A loss to the 49ers certainly wouldn't dash the good guys' hopes for a division crown, as they'd still control their own destiny; it could, for all intents and purposes, slam the door on the coveted #1 seed in the NFC and its lone bye. The 49ers do have some tricky games left, but I'd think the only one they'd be underdogs in would be the Week 16 road game against the Kansas City Chiefs. At effectively two games behind, the Vikings would probably need to be flawless.

Kirk Cousins' return changes the formula dramatically. If the trade didn't happen, I'd give the Vikings offense little to no shot at hitting 20+ points here. Or maybe even 10. And this is with the knowledge that the 49ers defense is kind of living off its reputation a bit from the earlier Kyle Shanahan era.

Don't get me wrong, they're solid. But it's not the unit from the 2019-2022 Robert Saleh-DeMeco Ryans years. That said, new (and highly experienced) coordinator Raheem Morris has them playing well: 11th in points per game (21.8), 6th in total yards (308), 17th in pass yards allowed (226), 2nd in rush yards allowed (82.6), and 20th in 3rd-down conversion rate (41%).

The standout stat is their exceptional success so far at stopping the run. The Vikings average 108.6 per game (19th) but improved in both volume and efficiency last week with Cousins back – e.g., higher YPC (4.2) and seven rushing first downs. Avoiding 3rd-and-long situations will be crucial in this one. Nice gains on 1st and 2nd downs will also open up the play-action game, which I'm sure will be a focus for Kevin O'Connell. Keeping the 49ers defense on their toes will make an already average pass defense even less effective.

Many 49ers sites are focusing on the fact that Cousins already faced this defense a few weeks ago, and the results were…suboptimal: 216 yards, 1 TD, 3 INTs, and a 55.3 QBR. Does this matter? To a certain extent, I suppose. Does it mean history will repeat itself? Definitely not. Different play-caller, better offensive personnel, and almost certainly a more effective game plan, with that game on film to learn from. To be honest, I fully expect this to be a far more challenging unit to face than the Packers were a week prior.

If you're going to go there, it's only fair to point out that Cousins is 2-1 in regular-season games in a Vikings uniform against Shanahan's 49ers, averaging 286.7 yards passing in those games, with 6 TDs and 2 INTs for an overall passer rating of 99.4. One of those was a great home win in 2023, when he passed for 378 yards in a victory without Justin Jefferson against the then 5-2 49ers.

Again, all that aside, getting the running game going early in this one will be critical. Allowing Nick Bosa, Keion White, and company to go wild repeatedly on 3rd-and-long isn't ideal, even if the 49ers' overall pass-rush metrics (e.g., sack rate) this season have been at or below average.

Again, this is your main event of the evening. Strength vs. strength. The NFL's #1 scoring/EPA defense faces the #1 yardage/efficiency offense. We all know how historically dominant the Vikings' defense has been. I touched on it a few days ago. But with apologies to the Bills, Lions, and Bears, this will almost certainly be the season's biggest challenge.

The 49ers are formidable: 2nd in points per game (31.2), 1st in total yards (399.8), 7th in pass yards (255.8), 3rd in rush yards (144), and 1st in 3rd-down conversion rate (52%). The Vikings' rankings in those categories? 1st, 2nd, 2nd, 17th, and 3rd, respectively.

The number that stands out to Vikings fans is that we're 17th in rushing yards allowed per game. There are vulnerabilities, no doubt. This is especially true since it's no secret that Christian McCaffrey is the straw that stirs the drink for the 49ers offense. He's Brock Purdy's security blanket and among the most dangerous dual threats in the NFL. But digging a bit deeper into the numbers, I found that the Vikings are much better (7th) in run EPA/play, at around -0.10. That makes you feel a bit better, but the eye test (for me, anyway) shows the concerns are legitimate.

If the Vikings are effective at stopping the run on early downs, it will play right into our hands. What's unique about Flores' scheme is that it flips traditional schematic reasoning on its head. Logic would dictate that the more aggressive the defense, the more vulnerable a team becomes in the quick, on-time passing game. Instead, the Vikings did a good job of taking that away in the last matchup against Purdy and the 49ers in 2024. ESPN's Jason McCourty and Dan Orlovsky had a great discussion about it that's worth your time.

And here's another post from The Athletic's Alec Lewis that's just ridiculous.

In the end, Kyle Shanahan is 0-5 when Brian Flores is on the opposing sideline, whether as a head coach or a coordinator. 49ers fans will say he's due. It's only a matter of time. Vikings fans will counter that this is the best Flores unit Shanahan has ever faced. Who will be right? We shall see.

The unique wildcard here is the altitude issue in Mexico City and which team's differing methods will prove more effective. I'm not a biologist and have no clue which strategy is better. I'm sure there's science supporting both approaches; why would a team deliberately put itself at such a disadvantage?

What I do know is that after Week 1, the 49ers had the oldest average roster in the NFL (27.49 years). They've since traded for 34-year-old Keenan Allen and 32-year-old DeForest Buckner to play prominent roles. The Vikings have similar issues, specifically with Harrison Smith (37) and Kyle Van Noy (35). You can also note Kirk Cousins (38). But there's a fundamental difference between a quarterback taking three- and five-step drops and a receiver running routes and blocking all game. The same goes for an interior lineman battling in the trenches for four quarters. Depth and scheme comfort/familiarity on both sides of the ball will be important here. Expect more varied snap counts than usual after this one.

I'm cautiously optimistic about this game. Jordan Addison being out again isn't ideal, but it opens opportunities for a Jauan Jennings revenge game, and Myles Price and Tai Felton played well last week, too. The key for Cousins will be to avoid turnovers, and the way to do that is not to press, which I felt was a bit of an issue on his return last week. The interception that counted was one example, and the one that didn't, because his knee was fortunately down, was another. If he takes what's there, nothing more and nothing less, the Vikings offense will be fine. The name of the game is to keep the chains moving and to afford the defense time to recover. Unless there's an unexpected turnover barrage, whoever wins time of possession is likely the victor. That's always true, but it's even more critical given the atmospheric conditions.

On defense, it all comes down to scheming effective pressure on Purdy without committing more than five too often. Listen, Flores is going to bring the heat, but it wouldn't shock me if the blitz rate were slightly reduced from the record-breaking highs if it's not getting home early. Purdy isn't going to be easily rattled and, with time, will quickly make the proper reads to exploit the mid-15 to 20-yard range, where we can sometimes be susceptible. And, of course, having to account for McCaffrey, who can take it to the house from anywhere, on every play could even out the numbers game.

I've got a death grip on the positive vibes since Cousins' return, and I have no plans to let go until the situation warrants it. I want that bye week, and I think we'll stay on track to get it.

#2762. Mike Evans injury update: Will 49ers WR play vs. Vikings?

San Francisco 49ers wide receiver Mike Evans could be available against the Minnesota Vikings.

Evans first went down with Achilles tendinitis in Week 6 in the 49ers' win over the Washington Commanders. He left that game late in the first half and didn't participate in a practice until Thursday in the lead up to Week 11. His return on Nov. 19 was exactly one month from the Commanders game he exited.

Here's the latest on Evans ahead of the 49ers-Vikings matchup on Sunday Night Football.

Evans traveled to Colorado Springs after staying home on the team's trip to Dallas. He didn't participate in Wednesday's practice before getting limited sessions in Thursday and Friday. He's officially listed as questionable.

It sure looks like signs are pointing toward Evans playing. The 49ers released veteran WR Brandin Cooks on Saturday to make room for defensive tackle CJ West who came off IR. San Francisco also didn't elevate any WRs from the practice squad. Perhaps Evans is inactive with the five other WRs active, but chances are the club would've wanted Cooks on the roster still if they were worried Evans wasn't going to play.

Shanahan on Friday sounded optimistic when discussing Evans's week of practice and his status for Sunday.

"He had a good day of practice yesterday," Shanahan said. "As always, he's a little sore today, but going in the right direction."

If Evans goes he'll start. It'll be interesting to see if it's Deebo Samuel and/or Keenan Allen out with him. Allen has two weeks in the 49ers' system and had a strong five-catch, 61-yard performance in his debut. Jordan Watkins, Demarcus Robinson and Jacob Cowing are the other options.

#2758. Rafa rules at the Bernabeu! Tennis legend Nadal storms onto Real Madrid board with 99.5% landslide vote

Tennis legend Rafael Nadal has been overwhelmingly elected to Real Madrid's Board of Directors after receiving 99.5 per cent of the vote at the club's Extraordinary General Assembly. Joined by president Florentino Perez, the 22-time Grand Slam champion pledged to help keep Los Blancos at the pinnacle of world sport.

Tennis legend Rafael Nadal has officially joined Real Madrid's Board of Directors after receiving 99.5 per cent of the votes at the club's Extraordinary General Assembly. Held both in person and online at Ciudad Real Madrid's basketball pavilion, the vote saw 1,704 of the 1,713 representative members back the appointment, with just four against and five abstentions.

Nadal received a standing ovation from attendees as his new role was ratified under Article 46 of the club's bylaws. An honorary member since 2011, the Spanish icon now steps directly into the governance of the club he has supported throughout his life.

Addressing the assembly, Nadal described the appointment as a proud milestone that deepens his lifelong bond with Los Blancos. He stressed his determination to serve the club with humility while helping preserve its status at the top of global sport.

"It is a great responsibility to be part of the Board of Directors of Real Madrid," Nadal said. "This club is passed down from parents to children and unites people. For me, it connects me with family and friends to watch matches together. It's an immense joy and a great satisfaction. I am very aware of the responsibility because Real Madrid is a global brand. It is an infinite responsibility, and I take it on with the hope of learning and contributing so that this club continues to be the best in the world."

Real Madrid president Florentino Perez led the tributes during the single-item assembly, praising Nadal's character both on and off the court alongside his 92 career titles, 22 Grand Slams, two Olympic gold medals and five Davis Cups. Perez insisted the former world number one embodies the core values of the institution.

"Today is a very special day for our club and, personally, it is also very special for me as the president of Real Madrid," Perez stated. "And for us, there is something particularly special: Rafa is one of our own. He is an honorary member of Real Madrid, deeply feels these colours, and has always proudly expressed his madridismo."

Perez expressed confidence that Nadal's presence on the board will help shape the club for future generations while protecting its identity and ambition. Nadal echoed that long-term vision as he closed his speech to applause from members.

"I will work to ensure that things continue as they are: a successful club that remains so for years to come," Nadal declared before signing off with "Hala Madrid!"

#2757. NFC North continues its historic dominance

The NFC North continues to perform like no other division in the league.

Three of the teams (Lions, Bears, Vikings) have a record of 7-2 through 10 weeks. The Packers are 6-4.

The combined record of 27-10 equates to a success rate of 73 percent — the highest by any division through Week 10 in NFL history.

Outside the division, the teams of the NFC North are 21-4. The 84-percent success rate is the most for any division through Week 10 since the AFL-NFL merger.

This weekend, the Lions host the Bucs, the Bears host the Saints, and the Vikings face the 49ers in Mexico City.

In theory, all four teams could make the playoffs. That would require the fourth-place team landing higher in the standings than the second-place team in each of the other three divisions.

And that makes Wednesday night's game between the 6-4 Packers and 6-4 Rams critical to the outcome of the chase for the wild-card berths in the NFC.

#2756. Aidan Hutchinson wasn't fined for sack that injured Drake Maye

As expected, Lions defensive end Aidan Hutchinson wasn’t fined for the tackle that may have ended Patriots quarterback Drake Maye’s season.

The weekly announcement from the league includes no fines against Hutchinson.

A question emerged as to whether Hutchinson’s sack of Maye amounted to a hip-drop tackle. During the broadcast, Fox rules analyst (and former NFL senior V.P. of officiating) Mike Pereira said it wasn’t a hip-drop tackle. On Monday, NFL in-house rules analyst Walt Anderson confirmed that it wasn’t a hip-drop tackle.

But it’s not Anderson who makes the decisions on fines. Jon Runyan makes those decisions. Not surprisingly, Runyan decided that the tackle was not a violation of the rules.

Under the rules, a hip-drop tackle happens when a player “grabs the runner with one or both hands or wraps the runner with both arms; and . . . unweights himself by swiveling and dropping his hips and/or lower body, landing on and/or trapping the runner’s leg(s) at orbelow the knee.” Anderson said Hutchinson did not “unweight” himself or “swivel.”

Hutchinson suffered a torn ACL in his knee while celebrating the sack. Although it’s not clear whether Maye will play again in 2026, Hutchinson is done for the year.
